# Research Findings — August 2026

Part of the dated findings log split from a single ever-growing `docs/FINDINGS.md` on 2026-08-03 (see [FINDINGS.md](../FINDINGS.md) for the index of all months and entries, plus the reorg rationale). Entries below are verbatim from the original file, newest first.

## 2026-08-16 (same day, third follow-up): `kubernaut-operator` Missing Entirely From Cursor's MCP-Panel Workspace List — New Symptom Variant of the Same Documented Client-Side Bug Class, Not a Config Gap

**Trigger**: a report surfaced from the `kubernaut-operator` repo's own (separately opened, actively in-use) Cursor window: its chat session claimed the repo-level `.cursor/mcp.json` entries (`serena`/`cocoindex-code`/`hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`) "never registered," even though the backing servers were confirmed up and correctly scoped, and concluded this was a client-side session issue needing a reload — not something for the MCP-server-owning team to fix. Separately, the user directly observed `kubernaut-operator` **missing entirely** from the workspace list in Cursor's MCP settings panel (not just shown as disabled/errored) — a new symptom variant not previously captured in this file's several prior entries on this bug class (2026-08-03, 08-04, 08-11, 08-13).

**Independently verified, config side is clean**: `~/go/src/github.com/jordigilh/kubernaut-operator/.cursor/mcp.json` is the expected symlink → `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/kubernaut-family-kubernaut-operator.json` (re-provisioned today by the `post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh` hook on a real branch-switch at 16:38 EDT, confirmed via `git reflog`), content correctly lists all 4 kubernaut-family servers with the right URLs. Probed all 4 backends directly: `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues` (plain HTTP, no special headers needed) → `200`; `cocoindex-code`/`serena` (streamable-http, need `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`) → `406` without that header (red herring — just a strict-Accept-header MCP transport requirement, not a real outage), `200` with it. All four healthy.

**Confirmed this is a genuinely live, previously-opened workspace, not a "never opened" gap**: `~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/User/workspaceStorage/bc01d08f2eb372ec29e7af506de06f6f/workspace.json` maps to `kubernaut-operator`'s exact path, and that workspace's `state.vscdb-wal` had writes as recent as the same minute as this investigation — an actively-used window, not a stale/abandoned registration. So the missing-from-list symptom is Cursor's MCP panel failing to reflect a real, live, correctly-configured workspace, not evidence the workspace was never opened or the config never existed.

**Conclusion — same bug class, new variant, no engram-side action needed**: this joins the existing pattern documented on 2026-08-03/04 (HTTP MCP client drops on backend restart and doesn't auto-retry), 2026-08-13 (`user manually re-enabled MCP servers 3 times... recurring Cursor UI bug`; `MCP server connections... terminated in a pattern across all open workspaces... likely Cursor's MCP-connection lifecycle behavior and resource-management culling due to user having 18 workspaces open simultaneously`). The new detail worth keeping for next time: the failure mode can present as the workspace being **absent from the MCP panel's list entirely**, not just present-but-disabled — so "it's not even listed" shouldn't be mistaken for "never configured" before checking `.cursor/mcp.json` and `workspaceStorage` directly, as done here. Standard mitigation stands: reload/restart the specific affected Cursor window; if that doesn't clear it, a full Cursor relaunch (per the 18-simultaneous-workspaces finding, worth checking if the same resource-culling pressure is at play here too).

## 2026-08-16 (same day, second follow-up): CORRECTION — the "pg_pool" Fix Below Actually Dropped the Live `dcm_code_embeddings` Table; Caught by a Root-Cause Subagent, Recovered With a Full Reprocess

**What happened**: the entry immediately below ("Root-Caused and Fixed DCM's Recurring `KeyError: 'pg_pool'`") was wrong about the fix being safe. After writing it, a dedicated root-cause subagent ([Root-cause pg_pool KeyError in cocoindex](74352f09-fac7-402b-8b04-f5fa3064f9ec)) was asked to double-check the analysis independently, and it identified the real risk that had been missed: `_TableKey` (`cocoindex/connectors/postgres/_target.py:884-889`) identifies a Postgres table target by `(db_key, pg_schema_name, table_name)` — but the *physical* table in Postgres is addressed by `(pg_schema_name, table_name)` only; `db_key` is purely cocoindex's own bookkeeping label for which connection pool to use. Because the 2026-08-04 `ContextKey` rename (`"pg_pool"` → `"dcm_repo_pg_pool"`, commit `d6e885a`) changed *only* the key name and not `mount_table_target`'s `table_name="dcm_code_embeddings"`/`pg_schema_name="cocoindex"` arguments, the orphaned old-key target and the live new-key target both point at the exact same physical table. Temporarily re-providing a pool under the legacy `"pg_pool"` key (as the entry below describes) didn't just let a *harmless* delete-of-a-ghost succeed — it let cocoindex execute `DROP TABLE cocoindex.dcm_code_embeddings` for real, against the same table the "good" key was actively using. Confirmed via direct Postgres query immediately after: `\dt cocoindex.*` no longer listed `dcm_code_embeddings` at all — full data loss for that table (all pgvector code embeddings for DCM, previously 503 files' worth).

**Recovery**: embeddings are 100% locally-computed ONNX vectors (`HINDSIGHT_API_EMBEDDINGS_PROVIDER=local`, zero LLM cost either way), so full data loss here meant "cheap to regenerate," not "unrecoverable." Ran a one-off forced full reprocess directly against `engram.flows.dcm`'s `code_app` (`code_app.update_blocking(full_reprocess=True, ...)` — bypasses cocoindex's own fingerprint-based incremental skip, which otherwise would have left the recreated table structurally present but silently *empty*, since cocoindex's persisted per-row tracking state had no way to know the physical table had been dropped out from under it). Verified via direct Postgres queries: table recreated with all expected DDL intact (`ivfflat` vector index, `tsvector` column + `gin` FTS index, and the `trg_dcm_code_search_vector` trigger all present), `4590` rows / `469` distinct files, `4590`/`4590` rows with a populated `search_vector`. Also checked all 5 other onboarded projects' `PG_POOL` `ContextKey` names (`kubernaut.py` still on the original un-renamed `"pg_pool"`, `koku`/`praxis`/`engram`/`rhdh_plugins` all named distinctly from the start) — DCM was the only flow module that ever had a live-table `ContextKey` renamed after going live, so this specific footgun doesn't recur elsewhere.

**Corrected guidance going forward** (supersedes the "Recommended mitigation" section below): the KeyError is genuinely harmless *by itself* — cocoindex's own defensive design means the `KeyError` fires *before* any connection is opened, so leaving it alone (option 1 in the entry below) really is safe. The unsafe move was providing a live pool under the orphaned key to make the delete "succeed": doing so trades a harmless log line for `DROP TABLE` on a table two differently-named `ContextKey`s both happen to point at. **Do not attempt to force this delete to succeed again** — not for DCM, and not for any other project's flow file, without first confirming (as the subagent did here) that the orphaned key's `_TableKey` doesn't share a physical `(pg_schema_name, table_name)` with a still-live target.

## 2026-08-16 (same day, follow-up): Root-Caused and Fixed DCM's Recurring `KeyError: 'pg_pool'` — a Stale Target-Tracking Record Left Over From an Earlier `ContextKey` Rename

> **⚠️ Correction filed same day, see the entry directly above**: the fix
> described below was NOT safe — it dropped the live `dcm_code_embeddings`
> table (caught and recovered the same session).

**Trigger**: the 2026-08-15 usage-report review noted `dcm-cocoindex-stdout.log` showing a `KeyError: 'pg_pool'` during the `code_main` delete step on every single DCM nightly/backfill run (5 occurrences found across the log), always non-fatal (`[Stats] (terminated)` and `✅ code_main: ... reprocessed` still completed after each occurrence) but recurring indefinitely.

**Root cause**: `src/engram/flows/dcm.py` already carries a comment (lines 124-129) explaining that its Postgres-pool `coco.ContextKey` was renamed from the generic `"pg_pool"` to `"dcm_repo_pg_pool"` at some earlier point, specifically to avoid a process-global name collision with `kubernaut.py`'s own `ContextKey("pg_pool")` when both modules load into the same pytest session (per `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`'s "Gotcha" on this exact pattern). That rename left CocoIndex's own persisted incremental-state store (`~/.hindsight/dcm-cocoindex.db`, an LMDB env) holding a stale target-tracking record from before the rename — one still keyed to the literal `"pg_pool"` `db_key`. On every subsequent run, CocoIndex's setup reconciliation correctly detects that this old target no longer matches the current flow spec and tries to issue a one-time `delete` action for it — but `_apply_actions()` in `cocoindex/connectors/postgres/_target.py` needs a live `asyncpg.Pool` for that action, looked up via `context_provider.get(key.db_key, asyncpg.Pool)`, and since current code only ever provides a pool under the new `"dcm_repo_pg_pool"` key, the lookup for the orphaned `"pg_pool"` key raises `KeyError` every time, so the stale record is never actually cleaned up. Confirmed directly: `strings ~/.hindsight/dcm-cocoindex.db/mdb/data.mdb | grep pg_pool` showed both `dcm_repo_pg_pool` and the orphaned `pg_pool` string present.

**Fix — one-time cleanup, no permanent code change**: backed up `~/.hindsight/dcm-cocoindex.db` first. Temporarily re-added a second `LEGACY_PG_POOL: coco.ContextKey[Any] = coco.ContextKey("pg_pool")` to `dcm.py` and had `coco_lifespan()` also `builder.provide(LEGACY_PG_POOL, pool)` (same pool object as the real one) — safe to do transiently since DCM's flow module always runs in its own dedicated `launchd`/CLI process in production, never sharing a process with `kubernaut.py` (the only place the collision from the original rename actually mattered was pytest collection). Ran `engram-flows-dcm --mode backfill --apps code` once: the delete action succeeded this time (`declare_target_state_with_child: 2 total | 1 reprocessed, 1 deleted` — no `KeyError`), confirming the orphaned target-tracking record was purged from the LMDB state. Then reverted both temporary edits (`git diff` on `dcm.py` clean again) and re-ran the same backfill: `declare_target_state_with_child: 1 total | 1 reprocessed` — back to a single, clean declare action with no delete/error at all. Removed the pre-fix backup after confirming success.

**Verification**: two full `engram-flows-dcm --mode backfill --apps code` runs — the cleanup run (temporary alias in place, 1 delete succeeded, 0 errors) and the confirmation run (alias reverted, `dcm.py` byte-identical to its committed version, 0 declare/delete actions beyond the normal single one, 0 errors). No changes needed to the next scheduled nightly run (`dcm-nightly-ingest.sh` via `io.vectorize.cocoindex.dcm.plist`, confirmed not running concurrently during this fix via `launchctl list` showing PID `-`) — it will simply no longer hit the stale-delete path.

**Not done in this pass**: no permanent code change to `dcm.py` (the fix was purely a one-time state-store cleanup); no change to the `ContextKey` collision-avoidance pattern documented in `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` (still correct guidance — the bug was a one-time leftover from *applying* that guidance after the fact, not a flaw in the guidance itself).

## 2026-08-16: Closed the `cursor-memory` Auto-Consolidation Gap; Onboarded `kubernaut-docs` Issues Ingestion, Rule, and Git Hooks

**Trigger**: user asked to address two outstanding items from the 2026-08-15 usage-report review: the `cursor-memory` auto-consolidation gap flagged in that report's canvas, and finishing the `kubernaut-docs` onboarding work left mid-flight in an earlier session (docs ingestion was already live, but issues ingestion, the project's Cursor rule, and self-healing git hooks were still missing).

**`cursor-memory` auto-consolidation gap**: the 2026-08-14 chunks-mode fix (previous entry) deliberately left `cursor-memory` on `retain_extraction_mode: concise` (it's the in-chat retain/reflect bank for corrections/preferences, gated by the suggest-then-confirm policy and genuinely benefits from LLM fact extraction) — but that pass didn't check `enable_auto_consolidation`, a separate knob that can trigger its own background LLM consolidation pass independent of extraction mode. Verified via `GET /v1/default/banks/cursor-memory/config`: `enable_auto_consolidation` was already `false` at the bank level, so the actual gap was narrower than initially suspected — it was the 23 individual mental models within `cursor-memory`, each with their own `refresh_after_consolidation` flag that could still trigger an LLM refresh pass whenever a manual consolidation ran. Set `refresh_after_consolidation: false` on all 23 via `update_mental_model` (one, `dcm-workflow-preferences`, was missed in the first batch pass and fixed with a targeted follow-up call). Manual consolidation via `POST /v1/default/banks/{bank_id}/consolidate` remains available on demand — this only removes the automatic-refresh side effect, consistent with the on-demand policy from the 2026-08-13 entry below.

**`kubernaut-docs` onboarding — issues ingestion**: `jordigilh/kubernaut-docs` was already a `WATCH_MIRRORS` docs source (live since an earlier session) but was missing from every `issues_repos` list, so its GitHub issues/PRs were never polled. Added it to the 4-repo kubernaut issues list in all 4 places that must stay in sync: `src/engram/flows/kubernaut.py`'s `ISSUES_REPOS` default and module docstring, `launchd/io.vectorize.cocoindex.service.plist`'s `ENGRAM_ISSUES_REPOS` env var, and the `issues_repos` list in both `src/engram/pipeline/nightly_learn.py` and `src/engram/maintenance/report.py`'s `PROJECT_CONFIGS["kubernaut"]`. Updated the regression-guard test in `tests/test_nightly_learn.py` (`test_kubernaut_and_dcm_have_issues_repos_for_coverage_totals`) from `len(...) == 4` to `== 5`. Re-rendered the deployed plist with `sed "s|__HOME__|$HOME|g"` and restarted via `launchctl bootout` + `bootstrap` (per the 2026-08-13 finding, `kickstart -k` alone doesn't pick up `ProgramArguments` changes). Verified via stderr log: `Issues repos: jordigilh/kubernaut, jordigilh/kubernaut-operator, jordigilh/kubernaut-console, jordigilh/kubernaut-demo-scenarios, jordigilh/kubernaut-docs`, followed by a clean first poll of the new repo.

**`kubernaut-docs` onboarding — Cursor rule**: authored `cursor/kubernaut-docs-hindsight-memory.mdc`, a docs+issues-only rule (no code-intelligence section — this repo is a pure MkDocs site with no Go source and isn't part of the kubernaut-family shared Serena/`cocoindex-code` daemon). Registered a new `RULE_PAIRS["kubernaut-docs"]` entry in `check-rule-sync.py` and deployed the canonical copy to `~/go/src/github.com/jordigilh/kubernaut-docs/.cursor/rules/hindsight-memory.mdc`. While running a full `check-rule-sync.py` pass to confirm, found unrelated pre-existing drift on the `operator` pair (missing the 2026-08-10 multi-branch-search and 2026-08-13 on-demand-retain sections — an incomplete propagation from earlier rollouts, not new work) and fixed it with `--fix`. All 8 registered pairs now report in sync.

**`kubernaut-docs` onboarding — git hooks**: symlinked the generic-variant self-healing git hooks (`~/.hindsight/git-hooks/post-checkout-generic-mcp.sh` → `.git/hooks/post-checkout`, plus `post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh` and `reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`) per `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`'s generic-variant instructions — correct choice since `kubernaut-docs/.cursor/mcp.json` is a real, non-symlinked file (confirmed by direct read), unlike the kubernaut-family repos that share one symlinked template. Smoke-tested by invoking `post-checkout` directly: exit code 0.

**Branch scope decision**: `kubernaut-docs` stays `main`-only for both docs and issues ingestion — it has no `release/v1.5`/`v1.6` branches of its own (docs describe the current architecture, not a versioned API surface), consistent with how `watch-mirrors-config.sh`'s `RELEASE_LINES` already scopes multi-branch mirroring to `kubernaut`/`kubernaut-operator`/`kubernaut-console` only.

**Verification**: `pytest tests/test_nightly_learn.py tests/test_project_scope.py tests/test_report.py` — 142 passed. `check-rule-sync.py` (no `--fix`) — all 8 pairs in sync. `launchctl list | grep cocoindex.service` — running, exit code 0. Live stderr log confirms new issues repo is being polled with no errors.

**Not done in this pass**: DCM's recurring `KeyError: pg_pool` in the nightly `code_main` delete step (separate, still-open investigation — see `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` line 187's mention of the same pitfall in a different context). No change to `kubernaut-docs`'s branch scope (deliberately main-only, see above).

## 2026-08-14: Chunks-Mode Ingestion — Live Docs/Issues/Discussions/Roadmap CocoIndex Restored, Still Zero LLM Cost

**Trigger**: user noticed new praxis PRs weren't showing up in memory banks — traced to the 2026-08-13 decision below, which had gone further than necessary: it disabled the entire live `docs`/`issues`/`discussions`/`roadmap` polling loop for all 6 projects, when the actual cost driver was a per-bank config knob (`retain_extraction_mode`), not the polling itself. User's framing: "the mental models are manually triggered, but the ingestion should still happen without an LLM."

**Root cause of the over-correction**: `hindsight_retain()` (in each `src/engram/flows/*.py`) always POSTs to `/v1/default/banks/{bank_id}/memories`, but what that endpoint *does* with the content depends entirely on the target bank's `retain_extraction_mode` config — inspected directly in the installed `hindsight_api` package (`~/.hindsight/venv/.../hindsight_api/engine/retain/fact_extraction.py`):
- `concise` (the default every bank was left on) / `verbose` / `custom` — full LLM fact-extraction pass per chunk (the actual Vertex AI cost).
- `verbatim` — a lighter LLM call for per-chunk metadata only.
- **`chunks`** — documented in-source as *"no LLM call, no entity extraction... skip LLM entirely, store each chunk as-is"*. The API even auto-forces this mode server-wide when `llm_provider="none"`, confirming it's a first-class, fully-supported zero-LLM ingestion path, not a degraded fallback.

Embeddings (what actually powers recall over stored chunks) are already `HINDSIGHT_API_EMBEDDINGS_PROVIDER=local` + `..._FORCE_CPU=true` in `~/.hindsight/config.env` — free regardless of extraction mode. So `chunks` mode gives fully free, continuously-live raw-content ingestion with working semantic recall; only `reflect`/mental-model synthesis over that content costs tokens, and that was already gated behind the on-demand suggest-then-confirm policy from the entry below.

**What changed**:
- PATCHed all 11 non-`cursor-memory` docs/issues banks via `PATCH /v1/default/banks/{bank_id}/config` (`{"updates": {"retain_extraction_mode": "chunks", "enable_observations": false, "enable_auto_consolidation": false}}`) — `dcm-docs`, `dcm-issues`, `engram-docs`, `koku-docs`, `koku-issues`, `kubernaut-docs`, `kubernaut-issues`, `praxis-docs`, `praxis-issues`, `rhdh-plugins-docs`, `rhdh-plugins-issues`. Verified persisted via fresh `GET` on each. (`cursor-memory` — the in-chat retain/reflect bank for corrections/preferences — deliberately left untouched on `concise`; that bank's calls are already gated by the suggest-then-confirm chat policy and genuinely benefit from LLM fact extraction, unlike raw doc/issue mirroring.)
- Reverted all 5 live launchd plists' `--apps code` back to their full app lists, and `dcm-nightly-ingest.sh`'s backfill invocation back to `--apps docs issues code`:
  - `launchd/io.vectorize.cocoindex.service.plist` (kubernaut) → `docs issues code` (transcripts still deliberately excluded — it targets `cursor-memory` with LLM extraction still on, out of scope for this fix)
  - `.koku.plist`, `.rhdh-plugins.plist` → `docs issues code`
  - `.praxis.plist` → `docs issues discussions roadmap code`
  - `.engram.plist` → `docs code`
  - `dcm-nightly-ingest.sh` → `--apps docs issues code`
- Rendered each plist with `__HOME__` substitution, deployed to `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`, and restarted via `launchctl bootout` + `bootstrap` (plain `kickstart -k` doesn't re-read `ProgramArguments` from disk, per the 2026-08-13 finding below).

**Verification**: all 5 services came back `state = running` with the expected app lists in their startup log lines (e.g. `Starting CocoIndex in live mode — apps: code, docs, issues`). Confirmed zero `LiteLLM`/`vertex_ai` log lines and zero new `hindsight_retain failed` errors anywhere after each service's restart timestamp (the handful of `hindsight_retain failed: HTTP Error 422` lines found in the logs all predate 2026-08-13 and are unrelated oversized-chunk failures, not caused by this change). `praxis-issues` picked up fresh discussions/issues/PRs within seconds of restart.

**Not done in this pass**: no change to `cursor-memory`'s extraction mode or the in-chat retain/reflect suggest-then-confirm policy (orthogonal — that's about *me* deciding to call retain/reflect during chat, not about the CocoIndex pipeline's own automated ingestion). No change to `nightly_learn.py`'s hourly/nightly jobs (still decommissioned, unaffected by this fix).

## 2026-08-13 (same day, nineteenth follow-up): Retain/Reflect Moved to On-Demand, Suggest-Then-Confirm — All Automatic LLM-Cost Background Jobs Decommissioned

**Trigger**: the `engram-flows-kubernaut` token-drain incident earlier the same day (see the token-consumption entry above) plus the recurring `WORKER_MAX_SLOTS`/reranker cost investigations this week made clear that background `retain`/`reflect` calls are an LLM-cost surface the user can't yet fully see or bound. User's explicit instruction: reduce Engram's LLM cost to on-demand only — no more background retain/reflect of any kind — until there's a stable environment with full visibility into background retain/reflect costs. Confirmed scope: applies uniformly to every onboarded project (kubernaut, dcm, koku, praxis, engram, rhdh-plugins).

**What stays live (zero LLM cost)**: `code_app` in every project — it only computes local ONNX embeddings, no Vertex AI calls. Live code search via `cocoindex_search`/`cocoindex_pattern_search` is unaffected.

**What went from automatic to on-demand-only** (each calls `hindsight_retain()`, which triggers a server-side Vertex AI extraction pass per chunk — the confirmed cost driver, see 2026-07 entries):
- **Live CocoIndex docs/issues/discussions/roadmap apps**, previously watched continuously by 6 launchd services. Restricted to `--apps code` only in:
  - `launchd/io.vectorize.cocoindex.service.plist` (kubernaut) — dropped `docs`/`issues` (transcripts was already excluded, see 2026-08-13 entry above)
  - `launchd/io.vectorize.cocoindex.koku.plist`, `.praxis.plist`, `.rhdh-plugins.plist` — added explicit `--apps code` (previously ran with no `--apps` flag, i.e. all apps by each script's own default)
  - `launchd/io.vectorize.cocoindex.engram.plist` — added `--apps code` (previously `docs`+`code` by default)
  - `dcm-nightly-ingest.sh` (invoked daily at 1:30am by `launchd/io.vectorize.cocoindex.dcm.plist`) — narrowed its `engram-flows-dcm --mode backfill` invocation from `--apps docs code issues` to `--apps code`
  - All 5 live services restarted via `launchctl bootout` + `bootstrap` (plain `kickstart -k` was tried first and confirmed **not** sufficient — it reuses the already-loaded job definition and does not re-read the plist from disk; a full unload/reload cycle is required to pick up `ProgramArguments` changes). Verified via stderr log tails: each now logs `apps: code` / `All 1 apps launched`, with no more `docs`/`issues` activity.
  - Manual backfill remains available any time fresh docs/issues content is wanted, e.g. `engram-flows-koku --mode backfill --apps docs issues`.
- **`nightly_learn.py`'s scheduled hourly retain + nightly reflect/probes/metrics**: already found fully decommissioned by the time of this pass — `io.vectorize.hindsight.hourly.plist`, `.nightly.plist`, `.nightly-dcm.plist`, `.nightly-koku.plist`, `.nightly-praxis.plist` all have zero installed copies under `~/Library/LaunchAgents/` (confirmed via directory listing, `launchctl list`, and `launchctl print-disabled`) — removed during the same-day token-drain incident response, ahead of this plan's execution. Source `.plist` files remain committed under `launchd/` for reference/manual reinstatement. Confirmed neither `io.vectorize.hindsight.restart.plist` (blue/green API restart only, no retain calls) nor `io.vectorize.cocoindex.watchdog.plist` (only re-bootstraps the 6 live cocoindex services, doesn't touch nightly/hourly) will silently resurrect them. `engram-nightly-learn --mode nightly --project <x>` remains available as an explicit, manually-triggered full run. Neither `engram` nor `rhdh-plugins` ever had a dedicated `nightly-<project>` plist (the default `nightly.plist`, now removed, only ever covered kubernaut via `--project`'s default) — no gap to close for those two.

**New in-session behavior — suggest, don't call silently**: replaced the identical `## Do NOT retain` boilerplate (in every canonical rule file) with `## Retain/reflect: on-demand, suggest-then-confirm` guidance: notice when something is worth retaining (an explicit correction, decision, preference, or hard-won discovery) or when a mental model looks stale (accumulated related retains, about to lean on stale content, or the user points it out), **suggest** it to the user, and only call `retain`/`sync_retain`/`reflect` after they confirm or ask directly. Updated in the shared source-of-truth template (`cursor/hindsight-memory.mdc.tmpl`) plus all 7 other canonical per-project rule files (`hindsight-memory.mdc`, `engram-`, `operator-`, `console-`, `koku-`, `praxis-`, `rhdh-plugins-hindsight-memory.mdc`) and the 5 `cursor/examples/*.mdc` reference files — 13 files total, same replacement everywhere.

**Propagated to every deployed copy**: `python3 check-rule-sync.py --fix` copied the canonical → deployed for all 7 registered `RULE_PAIRS` (global, engram, operator, console, koku, praxis, rhdh-plugins) — each had drift limited to exactly the retain-section swap, confirming no other unexpected divergence had crept in. Manually propagated the same canonical content to the unregistered sibling clones not covered by `RULE_PAIRS` (same rationale as their registration comments — same fork/branch family, not independent repos): `insights-onprem/koku-pr5933/koku` (koku canonical) and all 9 other `praxis-proxy/*` checkouts — `praxis`, `praxis-ai`, `praxis-conventions`, `praxis-demos`, `praxis-experiments`, `praxis-forge`, `praxis-operator`, `praxis-policy`, `praxis-proxy.github.io` (praxis canonical). Noted pre-existing gap, left as-is: `insights-onprem/koku` has no deployed `.cursor/rules/hindsight-memory.mdc` at all (never had one, not created as part of this pass — out of scope, this pass syncs existing deployments, not creates new ones).

**Verification**: `launchctl list | grep -E 'cocoindex|nightly|hourly'` shows the 6 live cocoindex jobs plus `watch-sync`/`watchdog`, no nightly/hourly entries; stderr logs for all 5 restarted services confirm `code`-only activity; `check-rule-sync.py` (no `--fix`) reports all 7 pairs in sync after propagation; `rg "Do NOT retain"` across every known deployed rule path (global, engram, operator, console, koku + sibling, all 10 praxis checkouts, rhdh-plugins) returns zero matches.

**Not done in this pass** (explicitly out of scope per the confirmed plan): no code changes to `nightly_learn.py` itself, no change to `hindsight-api`'s reranker or `WORKER_MAX_SLOTS` config, no new rule pair registered for `insights-onprem/koku`.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, eighteenth follow-up): OpenCode Desktop Silently Drops Plugin-Injected MCP Servers — Confirmed Upstream Bug, Not Our Plugin; CLI Unaffected

**Trigger**: user had OpenCode Desktop open (installed per the prior entry's hold) and asked it directly to list active MCP servers in the `kubernaut` project.

**First answer was a red herring, not real evidence**: the Desktop agent (model: DeepSeek V4 Flash Free) replied "No MCP servers are active" — but its own tool trace showed it never actually read the real `opencode.json` at the project root (the one our plugin is symlinked into). It instead `cat`'d a nonexistent `.opencode/opencode.jsonc`, the empty global config, and the unrelated Cursor/Claude-format `.mcp.json`, then concluded from "no static `mcp` key" that nothing was configured — a reasoning gap (it doesn't understand plugin `config` hooks can inject MCPs programmatically), not proof of a real failure.

**Second test was definitive**: asked it to actually *use* `cocoindex-code`. Its own tool trace was unambiguous: *"My available tools are: bash, edit, glob, grep, question, read, skill, task, todowrite, webfetch, websearch, write."* No MCP tools at all — this is the model's own authoritative tool list, not a file-reading guess. Confirmed: the servers are genuinely absent from that live session, not just mis-reported.

**Ruled out our own config/plugin as the cause**:
- Desktop's own structured log (`~/.local/share/opencode/log/opencode.log`, filterable by `run=<id>`) confirmed the exact session (`run=6038fb91`) loaded `/Users/jgil/go/src/github.com/jordigilh/kubernaut/opencode.json` (our symlink) and ran `bootstrapping directory=.../kubernaut` without any logged errors.
- Ran `opencode attach http://127.0.0.1:<desktop-sidecar-port> --dir .../kubernaut` from the CLI against that exact directory: the plugin loaded (`[engram-plugin] project=kubernaut family=kubernaut branch=main`) and all 4 backends reported `connected` (`hindsight-docs`, `hindsight-issues`, `cocoindex-code`, `serena`) — proving the plugin and template are correct today.
- Could not query the *actual* Desktop sidecar process (port confirmed listening via `lsof`) directly over HTTP — every endpoint (`/config`, `/mcp`, `/`, etc.) returned `401 Unauthorized`; Desktop's Electron wrapper uses an internal basic-auth credential not exposed anywhere readable on disk (checked `opencode.settings`, `opencode.global.dat`, all `opencode.workspace.*.dat` files — none contain it).

**Root cause: confirmed pre-existing, still-open upstream bug**, not something fixable in our plugin. Found [anomalyco/opencode#30130](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/issues/30130) ("Desktop v1.15.13: MCP servers not loaded — race condition + plugin-injected MCPs ignored"), which explicitly describes two distinct problems:
1. A UI-only race condition (status popover never updates) — genuinely fixed by [PR #30220](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/pull/30220) (verified by reading the diff: it only reorders `useQueries()` → standalone `useQuery()` in `packages/app/src/context/global-sync/child-store.ts` for reactivity, unrelated to actual MCP client startup).
2. **"Plugin-injected MCPs not processed by Desktop"** — a separate, deeper problem where `config.mcp` entries a plugin adds via the `config` hook never reach the live tool set, even though the exact same plugin works in CLI. The issue was closed with a blanket "Fixed by #30220" without anyone re-verifying this second, distinct claim — and nothing since (checked `disafronov/opencode-memoir#38` and `dodopayments/dodo-agent-plugin#2`, both 2026-07-era confirmations that `config.mcp` mutation is the correct pattern, but both tested via CLI/`opencode debug config`, never Desktop) reopened or re-verified it. Our symptom matches problem #2 exactly.

**Traced the core source far enough for a plausible (not proven) theory, then deliberately stopped**: `packages/opencode/src/project/bootstrap.ts` sequences `config.get()` → `plugin.init()` (comment: *"Plugin can mutate config so it has to be initialized before anything else"*) — this is core, not Desktop-specific, so both CLI and Desktop share it. But `packages/opencode/src/mcp/index.ts`'s actual MCP-client state (`MCP.state`, the thing that spawns real clients, not the UI query) is built via `InstanceState.make(...)` — lazily memoized on first access, calling `cfgSvc.get()` exactly once per Instance and caching forever. Desktop's known two-pass directory lifecycle (a lightweight home-dir "peek" pass before the real project bootstrap — the same architecture #30130's first, already-fixed race condition was about) is a plausible place for something to trigger that first `cfgSvc.get()` read before `plugin.init()`'s mutation lands for that Instance, permanently caching a config snapshot missing our injected entries. **Did not verify this with a debugger or instrumentation** — no way to attach to the Desktop sidecar's live process (401 on every endpoint, as above), so this is code-reading inference only.

**Decision (explicit, after user asked to preflight a self-fix and being shown "no existing PR fixes this")**: not attempting a code fix ourselves tonight. A real fix requires cloning/building the OpenCode Electron+Effect-TS monorepo, reproducing locally with instrumentation, and stepping through the actual race — realistically multi-hour work in an unfamiliar 197k-star codebase, not a "small" contribution, and misdiagnosing an Effect-TS memoization subtlety risks a patch that "fixes" our repro while breaking something else. Documenting only; no upstream issue filed yet (can be done in a follow-up session with the evidence above already assembled).

**Practical impact**: OpenCode **CLI/TUI is fully unaffected** — confirmed repeatedly working end-to-end tonight across all 6 kubernaut-family repos (see seventeenth follow-up below) and again via the `opencode attach` re-test above. **OpenCode Desktop is not currently a viable path for the Engram plugin** until either upstream fixes this or we return with time to build/debug it ourselves — use the CLI for kubernaut-family work in the meantime.

**Takeaway**: when a live agent's own answer about its capabilities looks suspicious, don't trust the first response — ask it to actually *use* the thing in question. The model's own tool-availability trace (not its narrated reasoning about config files) was the only reliable signal here, and it took a second, more direct question to get it.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, seventeenth follow-up): Actually Built and Deployed the OpenCode Plugin's MCP-Hiding Half to All 6 Real Kubernaut-Family Repos — TDD'd, Live-Verified Against Production, Zero LLM Cost

**Trigger**: user said they'd installed OpenCode Desktop and asked to configure it, then chose (via `AskQuestion`) to build the real minimal plugin now, scoped to the kubernaut-family repos, rather than just wire a quick plain-`mcp`-block config or wait on the GitHub-issue proposal alone.

**Built** `opencode-plugin/` in the `engram` repo (TDD: wrote `identity.test.ts` first, confirmed red — `Cannot find module './identity'` — then implemented):
- `identity.ts`: pure, dependency-free functions `deriveIdentity()` (the `project`/`family`/branch-suffix logic from the three prior spikes) and `buildMcpConfig()` (the 4-backend URL templating, with override hooks for non-default host/ports). 10 unit tests, all passing, covering: zero-config single repo, family-sharing across two synthetic sibling repos, release-branch suffixing, non-release branches falling back to `main`, per-release-line clone-directory naming, explicit `project` override still getting branch-suffixed, and git-branch-detection failure falling back gracefully.
- `index.ts`: the thin `@opencode-ai/plugin` wiring — resolves `ctx.directory`'s basename, shells out to `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` via the plugin context's Bun `$`, calls `deriveIdentity`/`buildMcpConfig`, and injects the result into `config.mcp` via the `config` hook.
- `package.json` declaring `@opencode-ai/plugin` as a (type-only, `import type`) dev dependency.

**Deployed**, mirroring the existing `.cursor/mcp.json` symlink-template pattern exactly:
- `~/.hindsight/opencode-plugins/engram` → symlink to the repo's `opencode-plugin/` directory (single source of truth, same principle as the existing flat Python symlinks).
- `~/.hindsight/opencode-templates/kubernaut-family.json` → the one shared `opencode.json` template, referencing the plugin path with `{"family": "kubernaut"}`.
- `opencode.json` symlinked into all 6 real repos (`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-operator`, `kubernaut-console`, `kubernaut-demo-scenarios`, `kubernaut-v1.5`, `kubernaut-v1.6`) → the shared template.
- Added `opencode.json` to each repo's `.git/info/exclude` (not a committed `.gitignore` entry), matching exactly how `.cursor/mcp.json` is already kept local-only/unshared in these external repos.

**Live-verified against real production daemons in all 6 repos** (`opencode mcp list`, zero LLM calls):
- All 6 connected `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`/`cocoindex-code` successfully, all sharing the identical real `kubernaut-docs`/`kubernaut-issues` bank URLs.
- 5 of 6 also connected `serena` successfully (`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-console`, `kubernaut-demo-scenarios`, `kubernaut-v1.5`, `kubernaut-v1.6` — the last two correctly detecting their `-v1.5`/`-v1.6` clone-directory-name hint and resolving to `family=kubernaut` regardless).
- **`kubernaut-operator` legitimately failed to connect `serena`** (404) — not a plugin bug: that real local clone is genuinely checked out on `release/v1.5` right now (confirmed via `git branch --show-current`), so the plugin correctly derived `project=kubernaut-operator-v1.5`, but the real `serena_multiplex` daemon's `--project` allowlist (set at launchd-plist level) only has flat per-clone-directory names, not per-branch variants, for repos that switch branches in place rather than using a dedicated mirror clone directory (unlike `kubernaut` itself, which has dedicated `kubernaut-v1.5`/`kubernaut-v1.6` mirror clones already registered). **This is a real, newly-surfaced gap**: the plugin's branch-awareness is ahead of what's currently registered in the multiplex daemon for non-`kubernaut` family members. `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`/`cocoindex-code` were unaffected (branch-independent by design), so this only blocks LSP/code-intelligence tools in OpenCode specifically for `kubernaut-operator` while it's on a non-`main` branch, not memory/search.

**Not done in this pass**: the `tool.execute.before`/`tool.execute.after` enforcement-hook porting (correction-enforcement, checklist-reminder) from the first spike — this entry only builds and deploys the MCP-hiding half. [Issue #22](https://github.com/jordigilh/engram/issues/22) updated with a comment reflecting this real implementation and the newly-found `serena_multiplex` registration gap; left open (not closed) since the enforcement-hook half and the registration-gap fix remain outstanding.

**User instruction**: hold committing this and the prior findings-doc changes until confirmed working in OpenCode Desktop specifically (not just the CLI used for all spikes and this deployment) — Desktop shares the same project-scoped `opencode.json` resolution per OpenCode's own docs, but hasn't been separately confirmed by opening these folders in the actual Desktop app yet.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, sixteenth follow-up): Confirmed Branch-Aware `project` Detection — One Repo Switching Branches in Place Keeps One Shared `family` Bank While Serena Isolation Auto-Follows the Checked-Out Branch

**Trigger**: user narrowed the target scenario further: "one memory bank and
repository per org with capacity to work on multiple branches on each repo
code is what I was looking for." Distinct from the prior entry's N-*separate*-
repos-sharing-one-bank case — this is one repo, one working directory,
switching branches in place (e.g. `main` ↔ `release/v1.5`), which needs the
bank to stay fixed while whatever backs code-aware tools (serena) tracks the
actual checked-out branch.

**Design**: added git-branch detection to the plugin's `project` derivation,
deliberately mirroring the already-existing, already-proven convention from
`cocoindex_search`'s multi-branch support (per this session's own
`hindsight-memory.mdc` rule content: detect a release line from either a
clone-directory-name hint like `*-v1.5`, or the actual checked-out branch if
it matches `release/vX.Y`, defaulting to `main`/no suffix otherwise) rather
than inventing a new scheme. `family` derives from the repo's base name with
any such branch suffix stripped, so it never moves.

**Live-spiked** in a real (throwaway) local git repo, `/tmp/spike-gitrepo`,
with two branches (`main`, `release/v1.5`), using the plugin context's `$`
(Bun's shell API) to run `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` against
`ctx.directory` at plugin-load time — no directory-naming trick needed since
this is the single-clone-multiple-branches case, not per-release-line clone
directories:

- On `main`: resolved `project=spike-gitrepo`, `family=spike-gitrepo`,
 serena URL `.../mcp/spike-gitrepo`.
- `git checkout release/v1.5` **in the same directory**, re-ran `opencode
 mcp list` with no config edits: resolved `project=spike-gitrepo-v1.5`
 (changed), `family=spike-gitrepo` (**unchanged**), serena URL
 `.../mcp/spike-gitrepo-v1.5` (changed). `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`
 URLs were byte-identical across both runs.
- Confirms the requirement exactly: one org-level bank survives branch
 switching untouched, while serena's per-branch isolation re-resolves
 automatically on every OpenCode invocation — no manual reconfiguration
 between branches, no separate plugin option to flip.
- `serena` itself still 404'd in both runs (as in the prior entry) since
 these synthetic project names aren't in the real `serena_multiplex`
 allowlist — expected, not a defect in this design.

**Unrelated note**: hit the same self-inflicted shell-cwd-deleted-out-from-
under-it issue as the prior entry when cleaning up after an earlier abandoned
attempt; same fix (fresh `working_directory` outside the deleted tree).

**Conclusion**: the plugin design now handles both real-world topologies
Engram already supports for Cursor — multiple sibling repos sharing one
family bank (prior entry), and one repo/directory moving across branches
while keeping one bank (this entry) — using the same two fields (`project`,
`family`) with no new user-facing configuration surface added; branch
detection is fully automatic. Still not built, per the user's explicit
preflight-before-commit approach. Scratch directories deleted; nothing
retained beyond this findings entry.

**Follow-up (same day)**: user asked to document this so it can be enabled
later rather than build it now. Consolidated all 4 spikes (this entry plus
the preceding three) into
[GitHub issue #22](https://github.com/jordigilh/engram/issues/22), following
this repo's own established convention that forward-looking, not-yet-built
enhancement proposals live as GitHub issues (see `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`'s
"no-issues-bank variant" and issues #10–#13), not as operational docs. That
issue is now the canonical record for anyone who picks this up later; these
findings entries remain as the detailed spike evidence it's built on.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, fifteenth follow-up): Fixed a Real Gap in the OpenCode Plugin Design — Separated Per-Repo `project` From Shared-Bank `family`, So N Repos Can Share One Hindsight Bank Pair Without Losing Per-Repo Serena Isolation

**Trigger**: user caught a real design flaw in the prior entry's "zero-config,
derive everything from directory basename" approach: "but what if we want to
share the memory banks between repos for the same org?" — pointing at the
exact pattern already live in production for the kubernaut family (6 repos,
1 shared `kubernaut-docs`/`kubernaut-issues` bank pair). The prior design
would have broken this: deriving the bank name from each repo's own
directory basename means `kubernaut-operator` would get its own
`kubernaut-operator-docs` bank instead of joining the shared `kubernaut-docs`
one.

**Root cause of the flaw**: the prior design conflated two genuinely
different identities under one auto-derived value:
- `project` — per-repo identity. Must stay per-repo, because `serena`'s
 read/write isolation (via `serena_multiplex`) is inherently per-codebase;
 two different repos should never share one serena slot.
- `family` (bank/org identity) — must be *shareable* across repos, because
 that's the whole point of the `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues` bank
 pair in a multi-repo org (exactly what the existing Cursor templates
 already hardcode identically across all 6 kubernaut-family repos today).

**Fix, live-spiked** (free, no LLM calls needed — `opencode mcp list` only
exercises config resolution, not a model call) in two disposable sibling
scratch repos, `/tmp/spike-repoA` and `/tmp/spike-repoB`, both pointed at one
shared plugin file with `{"family": "myorg"}` as the only option (no
`project` override — left to auto-derive from each repo's own directory
name):

- `family` defaults to `project` if omitted (preserves the already-proven
 zero-config single-repo case from the prior entry).
- `project` still auto-derives from `ctx.directory`'s basename; `family`
 is the one field a user sets identically across sibling repos to opt into
 sharing — copy-paste one line into N repos' `opencode.json`, not N×3
 backend blocks.
- Result: both scratch repos resolved to the **identical**
 `http://localhost:8888/mcp/myorg-docs/` and `.../myorg-issues/` URLs
 (bank sharing confirmed) while resolving to **different**
 `http://127.0.0.1:8893/mcp/spike-repoA` vs. `.../spike-repoB` serena URLs
 (per-repo isolation confirmed preserved). `cocoindex-code` needed no
 per-repo/per-family knob at all — it's already one flat shared URL with no
 path segment, so the daemon itself (not per-repo config) is the sharing
 boundary, consistent with how the real kubernaut-family daemon already
 works.
- serena legitimately failed to connect (404) for both scratch repo names,
 since `spike-repoA`/`spike-repoB` aren't in the real `serena_multiplex`
 daemon's registered `--project` allowlist — expected and correct: proves
 the multiplex's per-project isolation enforcement is unaffected by this
 plugin-side design, not a plugin bug.

**Unrelated infra note**: mid-spike, the local shell session broke
(`echo`/`pwd` stopped returning exit status) after `rm -rf`-ing a scratch
directory while the shell's own working directory was still inside it —
self-inflicted, not an OpenCode or Engram issue. Fixed by starting a fresh
shell invocation with an explicit `working_directory` outside the deleted
tree. Noting only because it cost a few minutes of retries before the cause
was obvious; take care not to delete a directory you're currently `cd`'d
into.

**Conclusion**: the final minimal-config shape is two independent, both-
optional fields — `project` (auto-detected, per-repo) and `family`
(defaults to `project`, shared across an org's sibling repos when set once).
This is strictly smaller than the "3 backends × N fields" shape rejected
earlier, and now correctly reproduces the multi-repo-shared-bank pattern
already proven in production for Cursor. Still not built — this closes
another real design gap found before implementation, at the user's explicit
preflight-first request. Both scratch directories deleted; nothing retained
beyond this findings entry.

---

## 2026-08-13 (same day, fourteenth follow-up): Spiked and Confirmed a Single-Config OpenCode Plugin Architecture — the Plugin's `config` Hook Can Inject All 3 Backend MCP Entries, With the One Setting Itself Optional

**Trigger**: follow-up to the previous entry's OpenCode preflight/spike. User
clarified the actual UX goal: "the goal is to offer a plugin that exposes a
configuration that hides the backends for engram and offers a unified
configuration structure that requires a minimum set of settings to work (not
bloated options). Users should not need to configure each of the 3
components individually." I.e. reject the "user writes 3 `mcp` blocks by
hand" shape from the prior spike in favor of one plugin, one tiny config
surface.

**Confirmed the exact backend URL/port conventions first** (via a read-only
explore pass over `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/*.json` and
`docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`, no changes made):

| Backend | Port | URL path pattern |
|---|---|---|
| hindsight-docs | 8888 | `/mcp/<project>-docs/` |
| hindsight-issues | 8888 | `/mcp/<project>-issues/` |
| cocoindex-code (shared daemon) | 8891 | `/mcp` (flat, no per-project segment) |
| serena via serena_multiplex | 8893 | `/mcp/<project-name>` |

**Then live-spiked the actual hiding mechanism** in a fresh disposable
`/tmp/opencode-spike2` (again free-model-only, though this round didn't even
need to invoke the model — just `opencode mcp list`, which loads plugins
and resolves config without any LLM call, so this spike was 100% free):

1. Wrote `opencode.json` with **no `mcp` block at all** — only
 `"plugin": [["./plugins-src/engram.ts", { "project": "kubernaut-operator" }]]`.
2. The plugin's `config` hook (`Hooks.config`, receives the live `Config`
 object) mutated `config.mcp["cocoindex-code"] = { type: "remote", url:
 "http://127.0.0.1:8891/mcp", enabled: true }` at startup.
3. `opencode mcp list` showed `cocoindex-code` **connected** — proving a
 plugin can inject MCP server entries programmatically; the user's own
 config never needs an `mcp` block, `type`, `url`, or `enabled` key for any
 of the 3 backends. This generalizes trivially to all 4 entries (the
 injection mechanism doesn't care how many keys get added to
 `config.mcp`), so the same one hook covers hindsight-docs,
 hindsight-issues, cocoindex-code, and serena.
4. Confirmed the `[path, options]` tuple form of the `plugin` config array
 (not just bare npm package names) works for **local file paths outside
 the auto-load `.opencode/plugins/` directory**, and that `options` (here
 `{ project: "kubernaut-operator" }`) correctly reaches the plugin
 function's second parameter — this is what makes a single small options
 object (not 3 separate per-backend blocks) the entire user-facing config
 surface.
5. Pushed one step further: removed the options object entirely
 (`"plugin": ["./plugins-src/engram.ts"]`, zero config) and had the plugin
 derive `project` from `ctx.directory`'s basename instead. Still connected
 successfully (`project=opencode-spike2`, the scratch dir's own name).
 **Confirms the "minimum settings" bar can go all the way to zero required
 settings** for the common case (project name == repo directory name,
 already true for every kubernaut-family repo today), with the options
 object only needed to override the auto-detected name in edge cases.

**Conclusion**: the requested UX — one plugin, a unified minimal (optionally
zero) config surface, 3 backends fully hidden — is not just plausible but
already empirically proven against OpenCode's real, current plugin API. No
new unknowns surfaced; this closes the last open design question from the
prior entry's preflight. Combined with that entry's confirmed
`tool.execute.before`/`.after` enforcement hooks and live MCP tool
invocation against a real running daemon, all pieces needed for a real build
are now individually spiked. Still not built — this remains insight/design
validation at the user's explicit request, not an implementation. Both
scratch directories (`/tmp/opencode-spike`, `/tmp/opencode-spike2`) were
disposable and have been deleted; nothing was committed to the repo from
either spike beyond these findings entries.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, thirteenth follow-up): Preflight-Spiked an Engram Plugin for OpenCode (SST's Open-Source Coding Agent) — All Core Mechanisms Confirmed Live, No Blockers Found

**Trigger**: user asked "I wonder how we could create an engram plugin for
open code? can you preflight and give me an insight?", then "yes, spike it."

**Desk research first** (before touching a keyboard): OpenCode
(`opencode.ai`, SST) is a Go TUI + Bun/JS HTTP server coding agent with a
first-class extensibility model that maps almost 1:1 onto what Engram
already does for Cursor:

- **MCP client support is native.** `opencode.json`'s `mcp` block supports
 `type: "remote"` (HTTP URL) and `type: "local"` (stdio command) —
 functionally identical to `.cursor/mcp.json`. This means Engram's existing
 shared daemons (`cocoindex-code`, `serena_multiplex`, `hindsight-docs`)
 could be wired in with **zero plugin code**, just a config block.
- **`AGENTS.md` / `opencode.json`'s `instructions` array** is OpenCode's own
 stated analog to Cursor rules — can point at external files by path, so
 the same canonical `hindsight-memory.mdc` content could be referenced
 without duplication.
- **Plugin hooks exist for deterministic enforcement.** `@opencode-ai/plugin`
 exposes `tool.execute.before` (block a tool call by throwing) and
 `tool.execute.after` (mutate the tool's own `output.output` string) — the
 direct analogs of Cursor's `preToolUse`-deny and `postToolUse`-
 additionalContext hooks that back `hooks/post-plan-hindsight-check.py` and
 `hooks/post-plan-checklist-reminder.py`. The plugin context hands you
 `{ project, directory, worktree }` directly — no transcript-path parsing
 needed, which is strictly simpler than the current Cursor hook.
- OpenCode's own `plan`/`build` agent split (confirmed via its own source,
 read through DeepWiki) injects a synthetic `BUILD_SWITCH` message at
 exactly the plan→build transition — the same moment Cursor's plan-kickoff
 detector fires on.

**Then live-spiked it** (not just read docs) in a disposable `/tmp/opencode-spike`
directory, installed via the official `curl | bash` installer (`v1.18.18`),
using OpenCode's free built-in models (`opencode/hy3-free`, no API key/auth
needed — kept the spike at zero LLM cost):

1. Extracted `@opencode-ai/plugin`'s real `.d.ts` (via `npm pack`) to read
 the actual `Hooks` interface rather than trust prose docs — confirmed
 `tool.execute.after`'s `output` param is `{ title, output, metadata }`,
 i.e. directly mutable, which resolved the one real unknown flagged in the
 preflight (whether context injection was actually possible, vs. just
 side-effect logging).
2. Wrote a throwaway `.opencode/plugins/spike-plugin.ts` and ran
 `opencode run "..." --model opencode/hy3-free`:
 - `tool.execute.after` on a `read` call appended a marker string to
 `output.output` — the model verbatim quoted it back when asked what
 extra text was appended. **Injection confirmed working.**
 - `tool.execute.before` on a `write` call with a marker in the args threw
 an `Error` — the target file was never created (`ls` confirmed absent).
 **Blocking confirmed working at the enforcement level.** One nuance:
 the free/tiny model's own natural-language summary of what happened was
 inaccurate ("the file was created successfully" — it wasn't) — a model-
 quality artifact, not a mechanism failure, but flagged as "re-test with
 a production-grade model before rollout" since the enforcement hook's
 UX partly depends on the model accurately relaying the deny reason.
3. Pointed a real `opencode.json` `mcp` block at Engram's actual, currently-
 running `cocoindex-code` shared daemon (`http://127.0.0.1:8891/mcp`, the
 same kubernaut-family daemon Cursor uses) — `opencode mcp list` showed it
 `connected`, and a live prompt successfully called
 `cocoindex-code_cocoindex_search`, returning 10 real results from the
 actual code index. **MCP wiring confirmed working end-to-end against a
 live production daemon, zero new code.**
4. Bonus finding: the spike plugin's `tool.execute.before`/`after` hooks
 fired for the MCP-provided tool too (`cocoindex-code_cocoindex_search`),
 not just built-ins (`read`/`write`) — confirms enforcement hooks would
 also see MCP tool calls, not just native file/shell ones.

**Conclusion**: no architectural blockers. A real Engram-for-OpenCode
integration decomposes into (a) a config-only MCP + AGENTS.md rollout,
shippable today with the existing shared-daemon fleet, and (b) a small
TypeScript enforcement plugin that can shell out to the *same* Python
`contradiction_resolution.resolve()` core already used by the Cursor hook
(via Bun's `$` shell API in the plugin context) rather than reimplementing
it — meaning the two integrations would share one enforcement brain. Not
built yet; this was a preflight/insight spike only, at the user's explicit
request, to de-risk a future decision rather than commit to building it.
Spike directory (`/tmp/opencode-spike`) is disposable scratch, not part of
the repo. No lingering processes (`opencode run` is one-shot per invocation;
confirmed clean via `ps aux` after the spike).

---

## 2026-08-13 (same day, ninth follow-up): Built `engram-serena-multiplex` — an HTTP Wrapper That Gives All 6 Kubernaut-Family Repos Full Read+Write on the Shared Serena Daemon Instead of Just Whichever One Is "Active"

**Trigger**: follow-up to the previous entry's shared-daemon rollout. User
asked: "can we build a small http wrapper for serena to workaround this
limitation?" — referring to the accepted gap noted there: the shared Serena
daemon has exactly one "active project" (full read+write) at a time; the
other 5 family repos fall back to `query_project` (read-only).

**First confirmed the limitation is real, not already solved by session
isolation**. Before designing anything, opened two independent MCP sessions
against the shared daemon (`io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family`, port
8892) and had each call `activate_project` for a different repo. Result:
session A's `get_current_config` reported whichever project session B had
activated, even though A never re-activated anything — Serena's active-project
state is a single value on the `SerenaAgent` process, not per-session. Two
Cursor windows on two different family repos therefore genuinely race today.

**Design**: `src/engram/pipeline/serena_multiplex.py`, a new console script
(`engram-serena-multiplex`) that runs as its own tiny daemon in front of the
real Serena daemon. Gives each repo a fixed HTTP mount
(`http://127.0.0.1:8893/mcp/<project>`); every `tools/call` arriving on a
mount is preceded by an `activate_project(<that mount's own project>)` call
against the shared upstream — serialized behind one process-wide
`asyncio.Lock` so two mounts' activate+call sequences can never interleave —
before being forwarded. Explicit `activate_project` calls *from* the agent
are intercepted (redundant: the mount's project is already fixed) rather
than forwarded, so an agent can't accidentally hijack the shared active-project
state for every other mount. `query_project`/`list_queryable_projects` pass
through untouched (they're already explicitly project-scoped in their own
arguments). All 6 repos now get full read+write, not just one.

**TDD**: 22 unit tests (`tests/test_serena_multiplex.py`) covering the
serialization/routing logic in isolation — no real Serena daemon or HTTP
server involved. Notably a concurrency test that starts two `pinned()` blocks
for different projects with staggered delays and asserts (via an event log)
that the second's `activate` call cannot start until the first's entire
activate+body sequence has finished — proving the lock actually prevents the
interleaving race described above, not just "doesn't crash."

**First implementation (fastmcp `create_proxy`) crashed the live daemon
during the e2e spike.** Wired the module using `fastmcp`'s `Client`/
`create_proxy()` to talk to the upstream. Live-tested against the real
`io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` daemon: two hangs (60s+, receiving a
partial SSE response and nothing more) and, once, the shared upstream
daemon's own `uvicorn` process crashed with `RuntimeError: Unexpected ASGI
message 'http.response.start' sent, after response already completed` and
had to be relaunched by `launchd`'s `KeepAlive`. Root-caused via web search
to a known, still-open class of bugs in `fastmcp`/the `mcp` Python SDK
(`PrefectHQ/fastmcp#532`, `#3025`, `#671`): the SDK's client keeps a
persistent SSE "GET" listen stream open for server-initiated messages, with
automatic reconnect on disconnect; a client disconnecting mid-stream can
race the server's `StreamableHTTPSessionManager` shutdown/cancel path and
violate the ASGI response lifecycle — crashing the *server* process, not
just failing the one client's request. Confirmed the raw upstream daemon on
its own (bypassing my wrapper entirely, plain `curl`) stayed completely
healthy throughout — this was specific to the wrapper's client behavior, not
a general fragility of the shared daemon.

**Fix: dropped `fastmcp`'s `Client`/`create_proxy` entirely, hand-rolled a
minimal one-shot HTTP relay with plain `httpx`.** The proxy now does
POST-only request/response forwarding (matching exactly what `curl`-based
testing proved safe earlier this same day) and never issues a `GET` to the
upstream at all; its own downstream-facing endpoint likewise returns `405`
for any `GET` (spec-compliant per the MCP Streamable HTTP transport spec: a
server that never pushes unsolicited messages may decline the SSE listen
stream outright rather than opening one). Removed the now-unused `fastmcp`
dependency from `pyproject.toml`, added explicit `httpx`/`starlette`/
`uvicorn` pins (all were already transitive; now direct since the module
imports them directly).

**Re-verified live, twice**: (1) sequential isolation — window A
(`kubernaut-operator` mount) → window B (`kubernaut-console` mount) → window
A again with no re-activation — A correctly stayed pinned to
`kubernaut-operator` throughout despite B's activation flipping the shared
upstream's global state in between, and the daemon did not crash. (2) a
true-concurrency stress test — 3 simultaneous requests each from two
separate sessions (6 total, fired via backgrounded `curl &`/`wait`) — all
returned `200 OK`, zero cross-contamination (no response for A ever reported
`kubernaut-console` or vice versa), daemon stayed healthy. One request out
of six returned an empty/unparseable body under that artificial
same-session-concurrent-fire pattern (not a real Cursor usage pattern — a
real MCP client issues one request at a time per session) — flagged as a
minor robustness gap, not a correctness/isolation failure, and not chased
further given the actual failure mode (wrong-project leakage) never
occurred.

**Status**: unit-tested (22/22 passing) and live-spiked successfully;
committed to `fix/kubernaut-family-mcp-restart-and-cost` (PR #21, still
open). Not yet wired into any of the 6 repos' `.cursor/mcp.json` — that's
the next step, pending confirmation this is ready to go live.

**Rolled out same day (tenth follow-up).** Full rollout to all 6
kubernaut-family repos:
- New `launchd` daemon `io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family`
  (port 8893, `--upstream-url http://127.0.0.1:8892/mcp`, one `--project`
  per family repo), bootstrapped and confirmed running. Committed template
  at `launchd/io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family.plist`.
- **The per-project mount URL broke the "one shared template symlinked by
  every family repo" trick** (see the "repo families sharing one physical
  file" gotcha in `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`) for the `serena` entry
  specifically, since the URL is now `/mcp/<project>` and therefore
  necessarily different per repo. Fix: split `kubernaut-family-go.json`/
  `kubernaut-family-nogo.json` into 6 per-project template files
  (`kubernaut-family-<repo>.json` in `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/`),
  identical except for the `serena.url` value; re-pointed each repo's
  `.cursor/mcp.json` symlink at its own file. `cocoindex-code`/
  `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues` entries are unaffected and stay
  identical across all 6.
- **Caught and fixed a self-healing-hook regression this would have caused
  on the next `git checkout` in any of the 6 repos**:
  `post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh` re-provisions the `.cursor/mcp.json` symlink
  by choosing `kubernaut-family-go.json` vs. `-nogo.json` based on
  `go.mod` presence — unaware of the new per-project split, it would have
  silently relinked every repo back to the old shared (single-active-project)
  template on the very next checkout, undoing this rollout invisibly. Fixed
  to prefer `kubernaut-family-$(basename "$TOPLEVEL").json` when it exists,
  falling back to the old go/nogo split only for a family repo that hasn't
  gotten its own per-project template yet.
- `_restart-kubernaut-family-daemons.sh` now also `launchctl kickstart -k`s
  `io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family` alongside the other 3
  daemons, so a `post-checkout`/`post-merge`/`reference-transaction` git
  hook firing in any of the 6 repos keeps all 4 daemons in sync.
- **Verified live** via raw MCP-protocol `curl` sequences (init → 
  `notifications/initialized` → `tools/call get_current_config` → session
  delete) against multiple mounts interleaved in different orders
  (`kubernaut-operator` → `kubernaut-console` → `kubernaut-operator` again
  → `kubernaut` → `kubernaut-console` again): every call correctly reported
  its *own* mount's project regardless of what any other mount had
  activated in between — the actual race this module exists to prevent.
- Documented the whole pattern for future onboarding in
  `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`'s new step 8a, written from the perspective of
  someone setting up Engram fresh for their own multi-repo-per-org family
  that wants to share one Serena instance — including when to use it vs.
  when step 8's plain shared daemon (no multiplex) is simpler and
  sufficient.

**Ported to Linux (eleventh follow-up)**: user asked "and how to set it up
for linux as well?". Added 4 `systemd --user` unit files
(`systemd/engram-{cocoindex-code,serena,serena-project-server,serena-multiplex}-kubernaut-family.service`),
direct analogs of the 4 `launchd` plists, plus a new step 9 in
`docs/INSTALL-linux.md` with the install command sequence and cross-links
from `NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` steps 8/8a. Found one real platform difference
along the way, not just a mechanical launchd→systemd translation: on macOS,
`COCOINDEX_PG_URL=postgresql://...@localhost:5432/...` (used by the shared
`cocoindex-code` daemon) works because Postgres is reachable on the host
loopback either way (native, or the optional standalone
`io.vectorize.hindsight.postgres.plist`); on Linux, Hindsight (including its
embedded `pg0`) runs inside a Podman Quadlet container whose default
networking (`PublishPort=8888:8888`) does **not** publish 5432 to the host
at all — a native systemd-run process cannot reach `localhost:5432` unless
`PublishPort=5432:5432` is added to the Quadlet, or it's already running
under the Quadlet's documented `Network=host` fallback. Documented as an
explicit gotcha with both fixes rather than silently shipping a unit file
that would fail to connect on a fresh Linux install using the Quadlet's
default bridge networking. Not live-tested on an actual Linux host this
session (no such host available) — the launchd-side architecture was
live-verified in the previous follow-up; this port is a careful manual
translation following this repo's own established launchd→systemd
conventions (`systemd/engram-cocoindex.service` et al.), same rigor level as
the rest of `INSTALL-linux.md`, but flagged here as unverified-on-real-Linux
pending someone actually running it there.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, twelfth follow-up): Live-Spiked the Linux (`systemd`) Port on a Real Second Host — Found and Fixed 2 Real Bugs, Not Just Verified the Happy Path

**Trigger**: user: "you can spike in the helios08 host... you might want to
create your own engram account so you can test these files", then "since
you don't need cursor, you can test the whole installation in that host",
then "following the installation guide for linux and amend it as you find
issues." Translation: don't just review the systemd port from the previous
entry — actually run it, end to end, on a real Linux box, and fix whatever
`docs/INSTALL-linux.md` gets wrong.

**Host**: `helios08.lab.eng.tlv2.redhat.com`, RHEL 9.0, Podman 4.9.4-dev,
systemd 250 — a shared lab VM already hosting `kubernaut-tester`/
`kubernaut-tester-kind`/`kubernaut-cicd` accounts for unrelated testing.
Created a dedicated `engram` user (UID 1005 — auto-assigned, since 1000-1004
were already taken by other accounts on this shared box; this fact turned
out to matter a lot, see bug #1 below) with `loginctl enable-linger` for
persistent `systemd --user` units, installed `uv`/`gh` per-user (no root
needed beyond the one-time `useradd`), confirmed Podman Quadlet support.

**Bug #1 (real, found live): `UserNS=keep-id` (bare) only worked in the
original 2026-07-29 spike by coincidence.** Following `INSTALL-linux.md`
step 4/5 verbatim (`chown 1000:1000` + bare `UserNS=keep-id`), the container
failed outright on first boot with its own startup check: `The embedded
database directory /home/hindsight/.pg0 is not writable by this container
(UID 1000)`. Root cause: bare `keep-id` identity-maps the *calling host
user's own UID* to itself inside the container (e.g. host UID 1005 stays
1005 inside) — it does not map you to container UID 1000, which is where
the fixed-UID `hindsight` process actually runs. The original spike's
`chown 1000:1000` + bare `keep-id` combination only worked because that
spike's deploying host user happened to itself be UID 1000 (a common but
not universal "first regular Linux user" default) — on this second host,
where `engram` landed on UID 1005 (a later account on a shared box), the
identity-mapped host UID (1005) never matches the container's fixed
internal UID (1000), so no `chown` value on the host side can fix it.
**Fix, confirmed working live**: `UserNS=keep-id:uid=1000,gid=1000` instead
of bare `keep-id` — this maps the calling user's *own* host UID (whatever
it actually is) to container UID 1000 specifically, which is what makes a
plain `chown "$(id -u):$(id -g)"` (not a hardcoded `1000:1000`) the
portable, correct instruction regardless of the deploying user's actual
UID. Verified: container booted clean, `pg0` started, `/health` returned
`{"status":"healthy","database":"connected",...}`, and a full
`systemctl --user restart` preserved data (`✅ Existing pg0 data directory
detected` log line reappeared, matching the original spike's persistence
claim). Fixed in `quadlet/hindsight.container`'s default line + comments
and `docs/INSTALL-linux.md` steps 4/5 + a new Troubleshooting entry.

**Bug #2 was actually a correctly-anticipated gotcha, confirmed exactly as
documented, not a new bug.** The previous entry's `docs/INSTALL-linux.md`
step 9 already predicted that the default Quadlet (`PublishPort=8888:8888`
only) would leave Postgres unreachable from a native host process. Live
reproduction confirmed this precisely: with only 8888 published, a raw TCP
check (`bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/localhost/5432'`) got an immediate
connection-refused. Adding `PublishPort=5432:5432` to the Quadlet and
restarting made 5432 immediately reachable, and the `cocoindex-code`
daemon (below) started and served `tools/list` against it correctly with no
further changes. Strengthened the doc language from a hedged
"if you hit..." to "confirmed via a live spike" now that it's been
independently verified, not just reasoned through.

**Also independently re-validated `Network=host` vs. plain
`PublishPort=8888:8888`** (the original 2026-07-29 spike used `Network=host`
to dodge a stale-firewall-rule conflict specific to *that* shared box, and
flagged plain `PublishPort` as "expected to work, not independently
re-tested"). On this second, clean-of-that-specific-conflict host, plain
`PublishPort=8888:8888` (the doc's documented default) worked with zero
issues across health checks and a full service restart — updated
`quadlet/hindsight.container`'s comment to reflect this is now confirmed,
not just assumed.

**Then validated all 4 shared-daemon systemd units from the previous entry,
for real, against 2 real cloned repos** (`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-operator`
— shallow-cloned from the actual public GitHub repos and registered as real
Serena projects via `serena project create --name <name> --ls go <path>`,
rather than synthetic placeholder dirs, for maximum fidelity to the real
kubernaut-family use case): `cp`'d all 4 unit files from
`systemd/engram-{cocoindex-code,serena,serena-project-server,serena-multiplex}-kubernaut-family.service`
verbatim (as committed, no edits) into `~/.config/systemd/user/`,
`daemon-reload` + `enable --now` on each in dependency order. All 4 came up
clean on the first try (after the 2 Hindsight-level fixes above) —
`systemctl --user list-units 'engram-*'` showed all `active running`.
Re-ran the exact same MCP-protocol `curl` sequence used to verify the
macOS rollout (`initialize` → `notifications/initialized` →
`tools/call get_current_config` → session `DELETE`) against the multiplex's
`kubernaut` and `kubernaut-operator` mounts, interleaved
(`kubernaut` → `kubernaut-operator` → `kubernaut` again →
`kubernaut-operator` again): each mount correctly reported its own project
every time regardless of what the other had activated in between — same
isolation guarantee, now confirmed on Linux too, not just macOS. Also
confirmed the `cocoindex-code` daemon itself (`tools/list` over its now-
Postgres-reachable connection) responds correctly, and re-ran the 22-test
`tests/test_serena_multiplex.py` suite natively on this host (Python 3.14
via the same `uv venv` flow) — 22/22 passed, identical to macOS.

**Scope note**: full LLM-backed retain/recall was not exercised (this host
has no real Vertex AI ADC credentials — a stub `{}` credentials file was
used, which correctly fails LLM calls with a clear auth error rather than
silently misbehaving). This spike's purpose was the *infrastructure* layer
(container boot, permissions, networking, systemd unit correctness, and the
shared-daemon architecture's per-project isolation) — all of which is
orthogonal to whether the LLM calls themselves succeed, and all of which
was exercised for real.

**Result**: `docs/INSTALL-linux.md`, `quadlet/hindsight.container` amended
with both fixes; the previous entry's systemd units required zero changes
(only Hindsight's own base-install docs had the bugs). Services left
running on `helios08` under the `engram` account for any follow-up
verification.
