## 2026-08-13 (same day, seventh and eighth follow-ups): Abandoned the Multi-Root Workspace; Replaced With Real Shared HTTP Daemons for `cocoindex-code` and `serena`

**Trigger**: after opening `kubernaut-family.code-workspace` (previous entry), user
rejected it outright: "doesn't work well for me, what the workspace provides is
not what I want. we can keep the enhancements we made but we'll have to create
a link for each repo and somehow hope that cursor can take it." Translation:
keep the *goal* (one shared MCP toolset instead of 6 duplicated ones), drop the
*mechanism* (multi-root workspace), go back to 6 separate per-repo windows each
with their own `.cursor/mcp.json` — but have that file point at one shared
long-running process instead of spawning a new one per window.

**Preflight/spike before rewriting anything** (explicit user instruction: "yes,
but preflight/spike it first to confirm both daemons actually work over HTTP
before rewriting all 6 configs"):
- Started `engram-search-kubernaut --transport streamable-http` and a `serena
  start-mcp-server --transport streamable-http` as ad-hoc background shell
  processes first — both died within seconds. Root cause: the Shell tool's
  session lifecycle reaps orphaned background children, which isn't
  representative of the real deployment path anyway. Re-spiked both via
  `launchctl bootstrap` of temporary plists instead (the actual mechanism
  these would run under permanently) — stable.
- `serena`'s spike initially failed with "Go is not installed", then "gopls is
  not installed" — `launchd`'s minimal default `PATH` doesn't include
  Homebrew (`go`) or `$GOPATH/bin` (`gopls`). Fixed by adding an explicit
  `EnvironmentVariables` block (`PATH` including
  `/opt/homebrew/bin:/Users/jgil/go/bin`, plus `GOPATH`) to the daemon plist.
  Confirmed after the fix: full MCP `initialize` → `tools/list` → real
  `find_symbol`/`cocoindex_search` calls succeeded over HTTP for both.
- User confirmed proceeding with full rollout after the spike passed.

**Rollout**:
1. Two permanent launchd daemons, `io.vectorize.cocoindex-code.kubernaut-family`
   (port 8891) and `io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` (port 8892), each
   with the corrected `PATH`/`GOPATH`/`HF_HUB_OFFLINE`/`COCOINDEX_PG_URL` env.
2. Rewrote `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/kubernaut-family-{go,nogo}.json`
   (already shared-symlinked into all 6 repos from the earlier 2026-08-11
   template rollout) so `cocoindex-code`/`serena` are `"type": "http"` pointing
   at those fixed URLs, instead of `command`/`stdio`. `hindsight-docs`/
   `hindsight-issues` were already `http` and unchanged.
3. Fully unwound the multi-root approach: deleted
   `kubernaut-family.code-workspace`, removed the 5 secondary repos'
   `.cursor/.no-mcp-provision` markers, and restored their `.cursor/mcp.json`
   symlinks (all 6 repos, including the primary, point at the same
   `kubernaut-family-go.json` — confirmed via `git log`-adjacent reasoning
   that `kubernaut-family-nogo.json` has in fact never been the target of any
   repo's symlink, despite the name; kept it in sync anyway per the existing
   "orphaned but maintained for consistency" precedent from 2026-08-11).
   Deleted `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/_effective-restart-target.sh` (the
   marker-path-derivation helper from the previous entry) — no longer needed
   once there are no more markers to derive from.
4. Replaced the per-repo-subprocess restart model in all 3 kubernaut-family
   git hooks (`post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh`, `post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh`,
   `reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`) with a new
   `_restart-kubernaut-family-daemons.sh` that does
   `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$UID/<label>` against the fixed daemon labels
   — simpler and more reliable than matching a process by cmdline substring,
   and correct regardless of which of the 6 repos' git operation fired it,
   since there's only one of each daemon now. `_restart-gopls.sh`/
   `_restart-serena.sh` (the old per-window matchers) remain used, unchanged,
   by the *other* families (dcm, koku/generic) that still run one
   stdio subprocess per window.
5. `kubernaut-demo-scenarios` had never been onboarded into Serena (flagged as
   a known gap in the previous entry — no `.serena/project.yml`, silently
   skipped at daemon startup with a `does not exist or no associated project
   configuration file found` warning). Confirmed it does have Go tooling
   (`tools/demo-http-server`, `tools/demo-operator`) despite no root
   `go.mod`, hand-wrote a minimal `project.yml` (`language_servers: [go]`)
   matching the format Serena itself generates, and added `.serena/` to its
   `.git/info/exclude` (it lacked a `.cursor/*`/`.serena/` gitignore pattern,
   same pre-existing gap as its `.cursor/mcp.json` tracking issue from the
   previous entry). Daemon restart picked it up; now appears in
   `list_queryable_projects`.

**Verification caught a real design flaw before it shipped**: end-to-end
testing (not just a connectivity check) revealed that starting the serena
daemon with `--project kubernaut` at startup — the natural-looking default,
matching the old workspace's "first root" — silently disables the
`activate_project` tool entirely (Serena's own "SingleProjectExclusions": any
single-project context, e.g. `--context ide`, that receives a `--project` at
startup assumes the client only ever wants that one project and never needs
to switch). Confirmed via a direct `tools/list` call: `activate_project` was
simply absent from the 16 active tools. This would have meant the daemon
could *only* ever serve `kubernaut` — the other 5 repos' `serena` entries in
`mcp.json` would connect successfully (no error) but be functionally
useless, a much worse regression than anything the multi-root workspace had.

Investigated two paths rather than accepting this silently:
- `--add-mode query-projects` (a built-in Serena mode) exposes
  `list_queryable_projects`/`query_project`, letting the *active* project's
  session read symbols/references from any *other* registered project
  without switching. But `query_project` hard-asserts
  `tool must be read-only` — verified empirically by calling `rename_symbol`
  through it, which was rejected with
  `AssertionError: Tool rename_symbol is not read-only and cannot be executed
  in another project`. It also requires a separate `serena start-project-server`
  process to be running (not the MCP daemon itself) — discovered via a
  `ConnectionError: ProjectServer is not reachable at http://127.0.0.1:24225`
  on first attempt; that server is a single shared resource (not one per
  queried project — confirmed by starting exactly one and successfully
  querying an arbitrarily-named registered project through it), so this adds
  one more permanent daemon, not five.
- Per Serena's own docs ("The Project Workflow" → "Multiple Agents Working on
  Different Projects": *"simply run a separate instance of Serena for each
  project"*) there's no supported way to have >1 simultaneously-active,
  full-read+write project on one instance. But omitting `--project` at
  startup entirely (rather than defaulting it to `kubernaut`) keeps
  `activate_project` available — confirmed empirically: with no `--project`,
  `tools/list` showed 20 active tools including `activate_project`,
  `rename_symbol`, `replace_symbol_body`, etc.; calling
  `activate_project(project="kubernaut-operator")` then successfully switched
  the daemon, and a subsequent `get_symbols_overview` call (no project
  argument) correctly returned kubernaut-operator's symbols, not kubernaut's.

**Final design**: the daemon starts with *no* default active project and
`--add-mode query-projects`. Whichever repo an agent calls
`activate_project(<path>)` for gets full read+write (find/rename/
replace_symbol_body/etc.) for the rest of that daemon's uptime or until
another `activate_project` call switches it; `query_project` remains
available as a read-only, non-disruptive way to peek at a *different*
repo's symbols without switching. Added a permanent
`io.vectorize.serena-project-server` daemon (fixed port 24225, not
kubernaut-family-specific — any future shared serena daemon can reuse it)
backing `query_project`.

**Accepted, documented limitation**: only one project can be "active"
(read+write) on the shared serena daemon at a time. If two Cursor windows
have two *different* kubernaut-family repos open concurrently and both call
`activate_project`, whichever call lands last wins for both windows — this
is Serena's own documented trade-off for "one instance instead of N", not a
bug introduced here. Documented in
`~/.hindsight/cursor-rule-templates/local-mcp-tool-preference.mdc` (shared
across kubernaut/dcm/koku/rhdh-plugins families, so written generically —
detect `"type": "http"` vs `"stdio"` in `mcp.json` rather than assuming
kubernaut-family specifically) with the instruction to `activate_project`
defensively at the start of a session/task, and to prefer `query_project`
for a quick cross-repo read that shouldn't disturb the active project.

**Net result**: 6 kubernaut-family repos, each still with its own
`.cursor/mcp.json`, but that file now points 4 of its 4 servers at shared,
already-running processes (2 hindsight HTTP endpoints, unchanged since
before this work; the new cocoindex-code and serena daemons) instead of
spawning 6× (`cocoindex-code`) + 6× (`serena`, each with its own `gopls`
loading all 855 packages) redundant stdio subprocesses. `cocoindex-code` has
no equivalent limitation to serena's — it was already fully multi-repo-aware
via `--repo`/`--branch` query params on every call, no "active project"
concept at all.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, sixth follow-up): Switched All Scheduled Batch LLM Jobs to Manual-Trigger-Only — Hourly/Nightly `nightly-learn.py` (kubernaut/dcm/koku/praxis) Plus an Open-Ended `prefilter-shadow-trial` Spike

**Trigger**: after the multi-root workspace consolidation above, user asked "did
you also disable any LLM or token consuming task? they need to be manually
triggered [only]" — i.e. not just kubernaut's `transcripts` app (already cut in
the third follow-up), but *any* automatic LLM/token spend, anywhere.

**Audit**: `launchctl list` + inspecting every `io.vectorize.*` plist turned up
six still-scheduled jobs making LLM calls, none of them examined by the
`transcripts` fix:

- `io.vectorize.hindsight.hourly` — kubernaut, every hour, `engram-nightly-learn
  --mode hourly` (Haiku correction detection + memory triage)
- `io.vectorize.hindsight.nightly` — kubernaut, 2:00 AM daily (mental model
  refresh, probes, contradiction-resolution escalation to Sonnet)
- `io.vectorize.hindsight.nightly-dcm` / `-koku` / `-praxis` — same `nightly`
  mode, their respective projects, staggered 2:30/3:00/3:30 AM
- `io.vectorize.prefilter-shadow-trial` — a spike from 2026-07-08 (see
  `docs/findings/2026-07.md`) calling Haiku on *every* new transcript message
  every 20 minutes to shadow-evaluate two candidate prefilter rules; still
  running unattended over a month later (9,385 log lines, ~4.2MB), with no
  promote/reject decision ever recorded.

Asked the user to confirm scope for both groups rather than unilaterally
reversing dcm/koku/praxis's automation (explicitly enabled at the user's
request on 2026-08-12) or killing an open-ended spike — user chose "all" for
the five `nightly-learn` jobs, then explicitly "stop" for the shadow trial too
once flagged.

**Fix**: `launchctl bootout` on all six, then moved their plists from
`~/Library/LaunchAgents/` to `~/.hindsight/launchd-disabled/` (archived, not
deleted) so macOS won't silently re-load them on next login/reboot — unloading
alone isn't durable across a restart. Each remains fully runnable by hand
(same binary/script, same flags) for whenever manual triggering is wanted;
moving the plist back and `launchctl bootstrap`-ing it restores automatic
scheduling if ever needed again.

**Deliberately NOT touched**: `hindsight-api`'s own internal worker pool
(behind `io.vectorize.hindsight.service-green`) — this is the always-on
mechanism providing real-time contradiction detection at `retain()` time and
delta-based mental model consolidation, which every still-running live
CocoIndex ingestion flow (kubernaut docs/issues/code, koku, dcm, praxis,
rhdh-plugins, engram) depends on to write memories at all. It's a different
category from the batch/schedule jobs above — it's also the exact thing fixed
for the delta-consumption bug and explicitly re-enabled-with-monitoring in the
2026-08-12 (same day, fourth follow-up) entry, not a candidate for reversal
here without a separate explicit decision.

**Not yet done**: no automated verification that all six jobs stay stopped
across a reboot — the archived-plist approach should be durable (macOS only
loads plists actually present in `~/Library/LaunchAgents/` at login), but this
hasn't been observed through an actual restart yet.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, fifth follow-up): Consolidated All 6 Kubernaut-Family Repos Into One `kubernaut-family.code-workspace` Multi-Root Workspace With a Single Shared MCP Server Set

**Trigger**: directly following the reconnect-cycling finding above, user asked:
"could we have one mcp suite for all kubernaut repos? like create a workspace
for all kubernaut repos and attach the mcps there so we only have 1 set for
all instead of repeating them? they should all share the same memory banks."

**Research before implementing**: dispatched the `cursor-guide` subagent to
verify current (Aug 2026) Cursor multi-root workspace + MCP semantics before
building anything, since guessing wrong here would have meant either broken
tool access or a silent regression. Findings, all sourced from Cursor's own
docs plus its community forum (not assumptions):
- Cursor does support VS Code-style `.code-workspace` multi-root files
 (shipped as "Multi-root workspaces in Agents Window", [changelog
 04-24-26](https://cursor.com/changelog/04-24-26)).
- The *intended* design merges every root's `.cursor/mcp.json` (project
 config wins over `~/.cursor/mcp.json` on name conflict, per
 [docs/mcp.md](https://cursor.com/docs/mcp.md)) — but Cursor staff confirmed
 on the [forum](https://forum.cursor.com/t/project-cursor-mcp-json-not-loaded-in-multi-root-code-workspace/167648)
 this week that **2nd-and-later roots' project-level MCP servers currently
 fail to connect at all** in a multi-root workspace (a genuine functional
 gap, not just a Settings-UI display bug) — only the **first** listed
 folder's `.cursor/mcp.json` reliably connects, or the fully-global
 `~/.cursor/mcp.json` (available in literally every window). A nightly
 build (3.16.13) reportedly fixes real connectivity but isn't stable yet.

Presented this to the user via `AskQuestion` before touching anything (3
questions: which workaround given the bug, which repos to include, whether
to fully replace the existing per-repo windows) rather than picking silently,
since the "first root only" behavior is an undocumented workaround for an
active bug, not a supported feature, and could change on Cursor's next
release. User chose: first-root workaround, 6 repos (the 4 issues-repos +
both release-line mirrors), and full replacement of the existing separate
windows.

**Why "first root only" is actually sufficient here, not just a workaround**:
neither of the two stdio MCP servers in play is single-repo-scoped in the
first place — this consolidation doesn't lose per-repo capability, just
per-repo *connections*:
- `cocoindex-code` (`engram-search-kubernaut`) already indexes the whole
 platform (kubernaut + kubernaut-operator + kubernaut-console +
 kubernaut-demo-scenarios, plus the v1.5/v1.6 release-line mirrors) from a
 single process, with `--repo`/`--branch` as optional narrowing filters —
 confirmed via its own `--help` output.
- `serena`'s `serena_config.yml` already had `kubernaut`,
 `kubernaut-console`, `kubernaut-operator`, `kubernaut-v1.5`, and
 `kubernaut-v1.6` registered as queryable projects (`list_queryable_projects`/
 `activate_project`/`query_project` let one running instance reach any
 registered project without its own process) — only
 `kubernaut-demo-scenarios` was missing from that list (never previously
 activated on its own), added it manually. That repo has no `.serena/`
 project directory yet (never onboarded) and isn't clearly a single-language
 codebase (mostly YAML/Helm/logs, no `go.mod`) — left as a known gap rather
 than guessing a `project.yml` language config; first real activation from
 within the combined workspace will trigger Serena's normal onboarding flow.
- `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues` were already shared (same
 `kubernaut-docs`/`kubernaut-issues` Hindsight banks across all these repos)
 even before today — this just stops opening N redundant HTTP connections
 to the same two endpoints.

**Implementation**:
1. Created `~/go/src/github.com/jordigilh/kubernaut-family.code-workspace`
 listing all 6 repos as folders, `kubernaut` deliberately first (the one
 whose `.cursor/mcp.json` — already a symlink into
 `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/kubernaut-family-go.json` — is the one
 that will actually connect).
2. Removed `.cursor/mcp.json` from the other 5 roots
 (`kubernaut-operator`, `kubernaut-console`, `kubernaut-demo-scenarios`,
 `kubernaut-v1.5`, `kubernaut-v1.6`) so Cursor doesn't attempt (and fail) to
 spawn 5 more redundant, non-connecting instances there.
3. Discovered while doing so that `.cursor/mcp.json` was actually **tracked
 in git** (not gitignored at all) in `kubernaut-operator`/`kubernaut-console`/
 `kubernaut-demo-scenarios` specifically — those three repos' `.gitignore`
 never got the `.cursor/*` pattern that `kubernaut`/`kubernaut-v1.5`/
 `kubernaut-v1.6` already have. Left this pre-existing inconsistency
 uncommitted/unfixed (out of today's scope, and fixing a shared `.gitignore`
 in someone else's repo isn't a call to make unilaterally) — the file
 deletions in those 3 repos currently show as unstaged `D` in `git status`;
 deliberately not committed or pushed, consistent with the standing rule
 that `.cursor/`-file changes in external repos are local-only.
4. Added a git-ignored `.cursor/.no-mcp-provision` marker file to each of the
 5 secondary repos, and taught the shared
 `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh` hook (kubernaut-family
 only — the same one from the 2026-08-08/08-09/08-11 entries above) to
 check for it and skip re-symlinking `.cursor/mcp.json` back in on every
 future checkout, without skipping the gopls/serena-restart or personal-rule
 provisioning it also does. Verified the marker actually suppresses
 provisioning via a direct dry-run invocation of the hook. For the 3 repos
 lacking a `.cursor/*` gitignore pattern, added the marker path to their
 (untracked, per-repo) `.git/info/exclude` too, matching the existing
 pattern already used there for `local-mcp-tool-preference.mdc`.

**Follow-up (same day) — the restart-staleness gap turned out to matter for
real, not just in theory**: user pushed back on the "known residual gap"
framing above, specifically asking whether this would affect the two
release-line branches (`kubernaut-v1.5`/`kubernaut-v1.6`) they're actively
capturing work on. Correct instinct: `kubernaut-v1.5`/`kubernaut-v1.6` are
live dev clones permanently checked out to their own release branch, kept
current via periodic `git fetch`+reset/pull refreshes -- exactly the kind of
ref-changing operation `reference-transaction`/`post-merge` exist to catch.
Without a fix, a mirror refresh in either would silently never restart the
shared serena instance, since that instance's only running process has
`--project kubernaut` on its command line, not `--project kubernaut-v1.5` --
reintroducing the exact class of staleness bug the 2026-08-08 finding closed,
specifically for the two branches most likely to need fresh symbol data.

**Fix**: added `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/_effective-restart-target.sh`, a small
shared helper that *derives* the correct restart target from a repo's own
`.cursor/.no-mcp-provision` marker — its content (previously just an empty
presence-marker) now holds the shared instance's actual root path. All three
kubernaut-family hooks (`post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh`,
`post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh`, `reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`) now run
`RESTART_TARGET=$(_effective-restart-target.sh "$TOPLEVEL")` before calling
`_restart-gopls.sh`/`_restart-serena.sh`, instead of passing `$TOPLEVEL`
directly. A repo without the marker (i.e. still standalone, or the primary
`kubernaut` root itself) gets `$TOPLEVEL` echoed back unchanged — verified
via dry-run trace (`sh -x`) of all three hooks against `kubernaut-v1.5` and
`kubernaut-v1.6`: each now correctly resolves and passes `kubernaut`'s path,
not its own, to both restart helpers. Confirmed via `grep` that this is
scoped to the 3 kubernaut-family hook scripts only —
`post-checkout-dcm-mcp.sh`/`post-checkout-generic-mcp.sh` (dcm/koku/other
families, still one-serena-per-repo) are untouched and still pass `$TOPLEVEL`
directly, exactly as before.

This generalizes cleanly rather than being a kubernaut-v1.5-specific patch:
adding/removing a family member later is just adding/removing its marker
file, with no hook logic changes. Retiring release/v1.5 support (user noted
it "should stop being necessary soon") needs nothing further here beyond
removing that repo's marker/registration whenever that happens — the
derivation logic itself doesn't know or care how many release lines exist.

**Also addressed**: the tracked `.cursor/mcp.json` in `kubernaut-operator`/
`kubernaut-console`/`kubernaut-demo-scenarios` flagged above as "left
uncommitted" — user asked to actually untrack it. Ran `git rm --cached
.cursor/mcp.json` (confirmed via `git diff --cached --name-only` that
*only* that path was staged, since each of those 3 working trees already had
unrelated pre-existing modified files that must not be swept into this
commit) and committed the removal in each repo — local commits only, not
pushed, per the standing rule that `.cursor/`-file changes in external repos
stay local. Also added `.cursor/mcp.json` to each repo's own
`.git/info/exclude` (alongside the existing `.no-mcp-provision`/
`local-mcp-tool-preference.mdc` entries) so it can't be accidentally
re-tracked by a future `git add -A`.

**Not yet done**: verifying this live inside an actual opened
`kubernaut-family.code-workspace` Cursor session (only reachable by the user
opening it — this agent's own session is scoped to the `engram` workspace
and can't open/inspect another window's live MCP connections). Recommended
next step for the user: open the workspace file, confirm `cocoindex-code`/
`serena`/`hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues` all connect once each (not 6×),
and close the old separate per-repo windows.

## 2026-08-13 (same day, fourth follow-up): `kubernaut-v1.5`'s "Disabled" MCP Flicker Traced to Cursor's Own Periodic Reconnect Cycle Across All 18 Open Workspaces, Not a Backend or Config Problem

**Trigger**: shortly after the CPU/LLM-cost fix above, user reported having to
re-enable MCP tools for `kubernaut-v1.5` yet again, and separately flagged (via
a Cursor "Needs Attention" panel screenshot) that `cocoindex-code`/`serena`
"appear briefly in a pattern search but fail on actual invocation ('server
does not exist')". Asked "what's going on?"

**Investigation**: ground truth for `kubernaut-v1.5`'s workspace
(`workspaceStorage/77a3f2124d.../state.vscdb`, `cursor/disabledMcpServers`)
was `[]` — genuinely nothing persisted-disabled, ruling out a real
approval-state problem (same check as the earlier "MCP Servers Repeatedly
Shown Disabled" entry below). Host CPU/load was also already back to normal
(load average ~5, no runaway processes) — the earlier `engram-flows-kubernaut`
fix had already resolved any resource-contention angle.

Read the actual per-server MCP connection logs
(`~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/logs/<session>/mcp-server-project-0-kubernaut-v1.5-{cocoindex-code,serena}.log`)
and found the real mechanism: **Cursor tears down and reconnects the stdio
connection for both servers roughly every ~2 minutes**, always right after a
routine `PingRequest` the server answers successfully (so it's not a
liveness/health-check failure) — e.g. `cocoindex-code` connected at 14:32:36,
got ping'd at 14:34:44, then "`stopped connection`" + fresh reconnect at
14:35:04; `serena` did the same at 14:33:09 → ping 14:34:55 → reconnect
14:35:05, essentially in lockstep with `cocoindex-code`'s cycle in the same
workspace. Grepping the full session's logs showed this "`stopped
connection`" pattern in **every** MCP server log for **every** currently-open
workspace (26 server logs across `kubernaut`, `kubernaut-v1.5`,
`kubernaut-v1.6`, `koku`, `engram`, `osac-service-provider`, etc.), including
plain `http`-type servers (`hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`) that have no
subprocess to restart at all — ruling out anything specific to
`kubernaut-v1.5`'s config, our git hooks (which only fire on git ref changes,
not on a ~2-minute timer), or the stdio spawn command. This is Cursor's own
MCP-connection lifecycle behavior, most likely resource-management culling
given the user had **18 workspaces open simultaneously** (`window1_wb0`
through `window1_wb17` in the session's log directory), each holding its own
set of live MCP connections.

**Why this looks like a real failure, not just a UI blip**: unlike a
lightweight `http` reconnect, `cocoindex-code`'s stdio process pays a real
cold-start cost on every one of these forced restarts — confirmed by direct
timing (`time engram-search-kubernaut --query ...`): **~6-7 seconds**,
dominated by Python/library import overhead (`torch`,
`sentence-transformers`, `cocoindex`, `asyncpg`) and lazy-loading the
`all-MiniLM-L6-v2` embedding model on first use (`_load_model()` in
`src/engram/search/kubernaut.py`), not network latency — a side-by-side test
with `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` (skipping the HuggingFace Hub cache-validation HTTP
round trips entirely) measured the *same* ~6-7s, since the model was already
fully cached locally (`~/.cache/huggingface/hub/models--sentence-transformers--all-MiniLM-L6-v2`)
and those HEAD requests were never the bottleneck. `serena`'s restart is
cheaper (~1-2s: `gopls` reinit + a fresh `go/packages.Load`) but not free
either. A tool call landing inside either window can genuinely fail or look
like "server does not exist" against the just-torn-down connection
reference, and Cursor's UI can flash "Disabled" for the few seconds until
the fresh connection finishes initializing — even though the persisted
approval state in `state.vscdb` is never actually touched by any of this.

**Fix applied**: added `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` to `cocoindex-code`'s env in all
four shared MCP templates (`kubernaut-family-{go,nogo}.json`,
`dcm-family-{go,nogo}.json` in `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/`) — cheap
insurance against the embedding model ever needing the network (it's fully
cached), even though it turned out not to be the dominant cost here.
Confirmed via direct CLI invocation that `engram-search-kubernaut` still
returns correct results with the flag set. Did **not** roll this out to the
~17 standalone (non-templated) `.cursor/mcp.json` files for
`koku`/`praxis-*`/`rhdh-plugins`/`engram`/`kubernaut-console`/
`kubernaut-demo-scenarios`/`osac-service-provider` — given the measurement
showing network calls aren't actually the bottleneck, the benefit there is
marginal (robustness against HF Hub being unreachable, not speed), so left
for a deliberate follow-up decision rather than churning 17 more
personal-only config files for a fix that doesn't address the real latency.

**No further backend/config action is possible or warranted**: the
reconnect cycle itself is client-side Cursor behavior outside this repo's
control, and re-toggling the "Disabled" switch when it flickers is a no-op
(it was never really persisted-disabled) that doesn't speed up the
in-progress reconnect. It self-heals within the same ~2-10s window every
time. If the flicker itself (not any real functional failure) is
disruptive, the only real lever is reducing how many of the 18
simultaneously-open workspaces are active at once, since each one keeps its
own full set of MCP connections alive for Cursor to cycle.

**Takeaway**: "the persisted `disabledMcpServers` state is empty" and "the
UI shows Disabled" can both be true at once without contradiction once a
third state exists — client-side reconnect-in-progress — and confirming
ground truth via `state.vscdb` (as the earlier entry below already
established as the right check) isn't sufficient on its own to rule out
*all* non-bug explanations; reading the actual per-server connection logs
was needed to distinguish "genuinely broken" from "mid-reconnect, cold
starting" this time.

---

## 2026-08-13 (same day, third follow-up): Runaway `engram-flows-kubernaut` Found Burning Haiku/Sonnet Tokens Continuously for 16 Hours — Only Kubernaut's CocoIndex Flow Has a Live LLM-Calling `transcripts` App, Now Cut to Manually-Triggered

**Trigger**: while investigating the MCP "Disabled" recurrence (previous entry
below), found the host under sustained heavy load (`uptime` load averages
~9-11 on a 12-core Mac) and traced it to `engram-flows-kubernaut` (the
`io.vectorize.cocoindex.service` launchd job) pinned at 107% CPU for ~16
hours straight — long enough to be starving other MCP servers'
handshake/startup and causing `cocoindex-code` for `kubernaut-v1.5` to be
genuinely disabled (the one real disable among the mostly-cosmetic set in
the sibling finding). User: "if that's consuming LLM tokens, we have to stop
it right now."

**Investigation**: `~/.hindsight/logs/cocoindex-stderr.log` showed a steady
stream of `LiteLLM completion()` calls alternating between
`claude-haiku-4-5@20251001` and `claude-sonnet-4-6`, both routed through
Vertex AI — i.e. real, metered LLM spend, not just CPU. Traced the call site
to `kubernaut.py`'s `transcript_app` (`process_transcript()`): every write to
a watched Cursor agent-transcript JSONL file runs
`correction_gate.is_correction()` (Haiku classification) on every new
message, and any message classified as a correction additionally triggers
`contradiction_resolution.resolve()` (which can escalate to Sonnet) against
the *entire* `cursor-memory` bank history for that project. This is
`live=True` CocoIndex watching, so it fires continuously and immediately as
the user's own agent sessions write to `~/.cursor/projects/*/agent-transcripts/`
— explaining both the sustained (not bursty) CPU and the LLM cost, and
directly implicating this very session's own transcript file as one active
contributor. Immediately stopped the bleeding by `launchctl unload`-ing
`io.vectorize.cocoindex.service.plist` per the user's explicit instruction,
confirmed the process was gone and the log went silent.

**Scope check — is this kubernaut-only, or does every onboarded project pay
this cost?** Grepped all six flow modules
(`src/engram/flows/{kubernaut,dcm,koku,praxis,engram,rhdh_plugins}.py`) for
`correction_gate`/`contradiction_resolution`/a `transcripts` app. **Only
`kubernaut.py` has one.** The other five projects' `--apps` choices are
exactly `{docs, issues, code}` (`praxis.py` additionally has
`discussions`/`roadmap`, still no `transcripts`) — none of them ingest
agent transcripts or do live correction detection at all. This was a
kubernaut-specific pilot feature (the original project, predating the
onboarding template used for every subsequent project) that never got
generalized — not a latent cost bomb sitting in five other services too.

**Fix — keep ingestion, cut the LLM-calling app, don't lose correction
detection entirely**: `kubernaut.py`'s `main()` already exposed a `--apps`
flag for exactly this kind of selective app startup (see `argparse` choices
`["docs", "issues", "code", "transcripts"]`); it was simply never passed by
the launchd plist, which defaulted to all four including `transcripts`.
Added `--apps docs issues code` to `io.vectorize.cocoindex.service.plist`'s
`ProgramArguments` (repo template + deployed
`~/Library/LaunchAgents/` copy), reloaded the service, and confirmed via
fresh log output ("`All 3 apps launched (code, docs, issues)`") and a
post-restart grep for `LiteLLM`/`correction_gate` (zero matches after the
restart marker, vs. thousands before) that ingestion resumed with zero LLM
calls. Also fixed a now-misleading hardcoded log string in `_run_live()`
that unconditionally said "docs/code/transcripts watching files" regardless
of `selected` — now reports the actual selected app set. Correction
detection isn't fully lost: `nightly-learn.py`'s existing hourly/nightly
batch pipeline (`io.vectorize.hindsight.{hourly,nightly}.plist`) still scans
transcripts for corrections project-wide on its own much-lower-frequency
schedule; only the *live, per-message, continuous* Haiku/Sonnet path for
kubernaut specifically is now off pending a deliberate cost/value decision.
To re-run it manually/backfill on demand:
`engram-flows-kubernaut --mode backfill --apps transcripts`.

**Takeaway**: a `--apps` selective-startup flag existing in the code is not
the same as it being *used* by the actual deployed launchd config — the gap
between "the capability exists" and "the plist actually passes it" let a
pilot-only, LLM-metered feature run unbounded in production for 16 hours
before CPU contention (not the cost itself) surfaced it. Worth an
occasional audit of what flags/apps each `ProgramArguments` array *actually*
requests versus what the flow module's `argparse` supports.

---

## 2026-08-13 (same day, second follow-up): MCP Servers Repeatedly Shown "Disabled" — Confirmed Mostly the Same 2026-08-11 Cursor UI Bug Recurring, Plus a Real, Independent Gap: `praxis-proxy` (10 repos), `koku` (3 repos), and `engram` Itself Never Got the Self-Healing Git-Hook Rollout

**Trigger**: user reported having manually re-enabled MCP servers "3 times today" for
"most of the projects," attaching a screenshot of Cursor's Tools & MCP settings
panel showing several servers red/"Disabled" — `cocoindex-code` for
`kubernaut-v1.5`/`kubernaut-v1.6`, `gopls-koku-service-operator` (User scope),
`hindsight-issues` for `kubernaut-v1.5` — while sibling entries for the same
tool on other projects (`engram`, `osac-service-provider`, `praxis-grid`,
`kubernaut`) showed enabled. Asked how to prevent recurrence.

**Investigation, part 1 — is the "Disabled" label real this time?** Queried
`cursor/disabledMcpServers` directly out of every open workspace's
`state.vscdb` (`~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/User/workspaceStorage/*/state.vscdb`),
the same ground-truth check used in the 2026-08-11 finding below. Result:
`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-v1.5`, `kubernaut-v1.6`, `kubernaut-operator`,
`kubernaut-console`, `engram`, `praxis-grid`, and `osac-service-provider` **all
show `[]`** — genuinely empty, nothing really disabled. Only one workspace had
a real persisted disable: `insights-onprem/koku` →
`["user-hindsight","user-gopls"]`, matching the exact same pre-existing,
intentionally-left-alone exception the 2026-08-11 finding already documented
for that same clone — not new, not related to today. **Conclusion: the
specific labels the user saw in the screenshot are, again, the same
Cursor-side UI-only stale-rendering bug from the 2026-08-11 finding below, not
a new bug.** Confirmed via web search this is a widely-reported, currently
open, upstream Cursor issue as of Aug 2026 (multiple Cursor community-forum
threads — "Disabled MCP Servers become enabled after each restart," "MCP
tools only available to Agent after manually toggling server off/on," "MCP
Server: Red error dot persists after successful reconnection") with Cursor
staff replies confirming "known issue... no permanent fix... team is aware and
working on a fix" — there is no config/settings lever on our side that
prevents it; `git config`, `.cursor/mcp.json` content, and hook installation
are all provably irrelevant to this specific symptom (installed cleanly on
repos with `disabledMcpServers: []` and the label still appeared).

**Investigation, part 2 — a real, independent gap found while checking.**
While verifying hook coverage as a secondary cause, discovered that the
2026-08-08/2026-08-11 self-healing git-hook rollout (which restarts a stale
`gopls mcp` / `serena start-mcp-server` process on `post-checkout`,
`post-merge`, and any HEAD-moving `reference-transaction` — see the two
findings below) was only ever installed for the kubernaut-family (5 repos)
and dcm-project (14 repos) clones. **Every `praxis-proxy` repo with a
`.cursor/mcp.json` (10: `praxis`, `praxis-ai`, `praxis-conventions`,
`praxis-demos`, `praxis-experiments`, `praxis-forge`, `praxis-grid`,
`praxis-operator`, `praxis-policy`, `praxis-proxy.github.io`), every `koku`
clone with one (3: `project-koku/koku`, `project-koku/koku-service-operator`,
`insights-onprem/koku`), and `engram`'s own working copy had zero of the
three hooks installed** (`git config core.hooksPath` + `.git/hooks` both
checked; engram uses `core.hooksPath=.githooks`, which had only the
pre-existing `pre-commit` hook). This is a real, if secondary, contributor:
without it, a stale `serena`/`gopls` process surviving a branch switch can
serve wrong-branch results or otherwise misbehave, which can look
indistinguishable from "disabled" to a user, and with today's heavy
branch-switching in `engram` during the Phase 8 cutover and `tags_match` work,
`engram` itself was exposed to exactly this gap all day.

**Fix**: added a new hook variant, `post-checkout-generic-mcp.sh` (alongside
the existing `post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh` for kubernaut-family and
`post-checkout-dcm-mcp.sh` for dcm-project) — same restart-`gopls`/restart-
`serena`/self-provision-`post-merge`+`reference-transaction` body as the other
two, but with **no** `.cursor/mcp.json` template-symlinking block, since
praxis-proxy/koku/engram's `.cursor/mcp.json` files are real
(non-symlink) per-repo files, not symlinks into a shared
`<family>-family-{go,nogo}.json` template — there is no such template for
these families, so (unlike the other two variants) there is nothing to
provision on that front. `post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh` and
`reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh` needed no changes at all — both are
already 100% generic (confirmed by dcm-project's `post-checkout-dcm-mcp.sh`
already reusing them unmodified). Installed all three hooks (symlinks) into
`.git/hooks/` for all 10 praxis-proxy repos and all 3 koku repos, and into
`.githooks/` for engram's own working copy (per `core.hooksPath`). The three
symlinks inside engram's own `.githooks/` point to absolute
`~/.hindsight/git-hooks/*.sh` paths (personal-machine-specific, same as
`.cursor/mcp.json`), so — matching that existing precedent exactly — they are
listed in `.git/info/exclude` (not `.gitignore`) rather than committed; only
this findings entry and the (portable, machine-agnostic) new hook script
itself under `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/` are the artifacts of this fix.

**Verification, without touching a real live process**: same methodology as
the 2026-08-11 fix — spawned a harmless fake process (a tiny `sh` script
literally named `serena`, invoked as `serena start-mcp-server --project
<scratch-repo-toplevel>` to match `_restart-serena.sh`'s command-line
substring match) in a disposable scratch repo, confirmed the new hook kills it
on a simulated checkout while every real, currently-running `serena
start-mcp-server` process for `praxis-grid`/`kubernaut-v1.5`/`kubernaut-v1.6`/
`osac-service-provider` (found via `pgrep -fl`) was left untouched throughout.
First attempt at this gave a false "not killed" result — traced to a test-
harness artifact, not a hook bug: macOS resolves `$TMPDIR` (`/var/...`)
through its `/var` → `/private/var` symlink, so `git rev-parse
--show-toplevel` inside the hook returned a different (resolved) path than
the unresolved one the fake process was launched with; passing the
pre-resolved toplevel to the fake process's `--project` argument fixed the
test. Real repo paths under `/Users/jgil/go/src/...` have no such symlink
layer, so this was purely a test-scratch-directory quirk, not a latent bug in
real usage.

**Not fixed (no fix possible here)**: the dominant, actually-reported symptom
(the red "Disabled" label itself) is an upstream Cursor bug with no local
workaround beyond what the 2026-08-11 finding already documented — a
**window reload** (`Developer: Reload Window`) clears the stale label for
every affected server in that window in one action, faster than toggling each
server individually one at a time (what the user has been doing, hence "3
times today" across "most of the projects" — each toggle-cycle is
per-server, not per-window). No Cursor setting, MCP transport choice, or
hook/config change on our side can prevent the underlying bug from
recurring; it remains open upstream (multiple live Cursor community-forum
threads, Cursor staff acknowledging "no permanent fix" as of Aug 2026).

## 2026-08-13 (same day, follow-up): "Cross-Project Pollution in Praxis" Traced to Every `hindsight-memory.mdc` Rule Instructing an Invalid `tags_match: "strict"` Value — the 2026-07-27 Isolation Fix Never Actually Worked for Any Project's `recall()` Calls

**Trigger**: user shared a screenshot from a `praxis-grid` session in which the
agent reported that a `praxis`-tagged `recall()` call "returned only stale
kubernaut/unrelated-project noise," attributed it to the documented "sparse
tagged content" limitation, and worked around it by grounding directly in the
codebase instead. User: "we have cross project pollution in praxis."

**Root cause**: every `hindsight-memory.mdc` rule (engram, dcm, koku, praxis)
instructs passing `tags_match: "strict"` for the cross-project isolation
guarantee established in the 2026-07-27 incident (see that entry). **`"strict"`
is not a valid value.** The real `hindsight_api` (`TagsMatch`) type is
`Literal["any", "all", "any_strict", "all_strict", "exact"]`
(`hindsight-api-slim/hindsight_api/engine/search/tags.py` in the
`vectorize-io/hindsight` fork). `_parse_tags_match()`'s `else` branch silently
defaults any unrecognized string to `"any"` behavior — which by design
**includes untagged memories** ("any": OR matching, includes untagged, kept
for backward compatibility). So every recall call that thought it was
isolating by project tag was silently running fully unscoped.

**Verified empirically against the live `cursor-memory` bank, three ways**:
1. `recall(tags=["praxis"], tags_match="strict")` → 25 results, **every one
 `tags: []`**, all kubernaut/DataStorage-flavored noise — reproducing the
 screenshot exactly.
2. `recall(tags=["zzz-nonexistent-tag-zzz"], tags_match="strict")` → nearly
 identical unfiltered results, proving `tags`/`tags_match` had zero effect,
 not even a soft ranking boost.
3. Re-running both with the **correct** value `tags_match: "any_strict"`:
 `praxis` → `[]` (correctly empty — zero facts are actually tagged `praxis`
 yet, `list_tags` confirms this), `engram` → 13/13 results correctly tagged
 `engram`. That's the isolation guarantee actually working, for the first
 time.

Mental models (`engram-testing-methodology` etc.) were never affected — those
are fetched directly by ID (`get_mental_model`), not filtered by this bug. The
4 praxis-scoped mental models onboarded 2026-08-10 do exist and are correctly
tagged `["praxis"]` (`list_mental_models` confirmed this); `list_tags`
initially appeared to contradict this by showing zero `praxis` tag bank-wide,
but that tool only aggregates tags on retained memories, not on mental
models — a real minor gap in `list_tags`, separate from the main bug, not
fixed here.

**Blast radius, found via `find -iname hindsight-memory.mdc`**: the wrong
claim appeared in 26 deployed rule files — engram's own, `dcm-project`'s 14
repos, `koku`/`koku-pr5933`, and `praxis-proxy`'s 10 repos — plus 2 of
engram's canonical templates in `cursor/` (`koku-hindsight-memory.mdc`,
`praxis-hindsight-memory.mdc`; `engram-hindsight-memory.mdc` was separately
stale — see below). `kubernaut-operator` passes `tags:` for the *separate*
`hindsight-docs` bank without any `tags_match` at all (inherits the same leaky
`"any"` default, just without a false "strict" claim — not fixed here, a
different design decision). `kubernaut-console` and the global
`~/.cursor/rules/hindsight-memory.mdc` predate the 2026-07-27 fix entirely and
attempt no project-tag scoping at all.

**Side discovery**: engram's own canonical/deployed pair
(`cursor/engram-hindsight-memory.mdc` vs `.cursor/rules/hindsight-memory.mdc`)
was already drifted independently of this bug — canonical still had the
pre-2026-07-27 "just ignore the kubernaut-dominated results" wording, missing
the tags/mental-model isolation section entirely. `check-rule-sync.py --pair
engram` correctly flagged this once probed. Fixed by treating the (correct,
current) deployed copy as ground truth and syncing it back to canonical,
resolving both issues in the same edit. `kubernaut-console`'s pair has a
similar unrelated pre-existing drift (confirmed via `check-rule-sync.py`,
which still reports `[console] DRIFT DETECTED`) — flagged here as a follow-up,
not fixed in this pass; it has no `tags_match` content to begin with, so it's
orthogonal to this finding.

**Fix scope, per explicit user correction** ("you can't push this to each
external repo: these changes are local to this user, not shared. The commit
should be to engram only"):
- Rewrote `tags_match: "strict"` → `tags_match: "any_strict"` (with an
 explanatory aside on why the literal string matters) and
 `with strict tag matching` → `` matched with `tags_match: "any_strict"` ``
 across all 26 deployed files plus the koku/praxis canonical templates, and
 brought engram's canonical template back in sync with its (corrected)
 deployed copy — all via direct file edits, verified with
 `check-rule-sync.py` reporting `engram`/`global`/`koku`/`praxis`/`operator`
 all "In sync" afterward.
- The **deployed copies in `dcm-project`, `koku`, `praxis-proxy`, and
 `kubernaut-operator`/`kubernaut-console` are local-filesystem-only edits,
 not git commits** — those repos are not engram's to push to. Only this
 repo's own two files (`.cursor/rules/hindsight-memory.mdc` and
 `cursor/engram-hindsight-memory.mdc`) plus the `cursor/koku-hindsight-
 memory.mdc`/`cursor/praxis-hindsight-memory.mdc` canonical templates (all
 inside this repo) are committed here.

**Not addressed (follow-ups)**: no canonical/`RULE_PAIRS` entry exists for
`dcm` at all (unlike koku/praxis/engram/operator/console) — its 14 deployed
copies have no drift-checked source of truth in this repo, a pre-existing gap
widened by this fix (14 independent local edits with nothing to catch future
drift); `kubernaut-console`'s unrelated pre-existing drift; `kubernaut-
operator`/`kubernaut-console`/global's `hindsight` (cursor-memory) bank calls
still have no project-tag scoping attempt at all, unlike the other 4 project
families — whether to add it is a separate design decision, not a bug fix.

**Takeaway**: a documented "fix" that specifies an invalid API parameter
value can look complete (a diff landed, a rule reads sensibly, a mental-model
split happened) while doing nothing at the layer that actually matters most
(ad-hoc `recall()` calls against the bulk of retained facts) — verify a
claimed guarantee empirically against the live system, with an adversarial
probe (a garbage tag that can't legitimately match anything) rather than
trusting that a parameter named the way you expect it to behave actually does.

## 2026-08-12 (same day, fifth follow-up): Restructured Engram Into a `src/engram/` Package With `pyproject.toml` Console Scripts, Then Cut Over Every Live `launchd` Service and All 35 External Repos' `.cursor/mcp.json` to Use Them

**Trigger**: user asked to lint the Python codebase and structure it better,
plus "what are the gaps for GA?" — engram had grown to 40+ top-level `.py`
files (flows/search per project, maintenance scripts, shared libs) with no
package structure, loaded at runtime via a bespoke `load_hyphenated_module()`
(`importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`) because filenames like
`nightly-learn.py` aren't valid Python identifiers. Chose the "full package"
option: `src/engram/` layout + `pyproject.toml` with `[project.scripts]`
console entry points, on a dedicated `reorg/package-layout` branch, explicitly
paused before the `launchd`/`.cursor/mcp.json` cutover (phase 8) until the
package itself was proven stable.

**Phase 0-7 (package restructure, merged via [PR #14](https://github.com/jordigilh/engram/pull/14))**:

- Phase 0-4: scaffolded `src/engram/` + `pyproject.toml`, moved 8 shared libs,
 10 flows/search files, 6 pipeline entry points, and 9 maintenance/admin
 scripts into the package, replacing `load_hyphenated_module()` call sites
 with real `from engram.x import y` imports everywhere except
 `hooks/_hindsight_check_worker.py` (deliberately kept on the flat-symlink
 `sys.path.insert` pattern — see the near-miss below).
- Phase 5: added 13 `console_scripts` entry points to `pyproject.toml`
 (`engram-flows-<project>`, `engram-search-<project>`, `engram-nightly-learn`,
 `engram-hindsight-proxy`, etc.), dropped `load_hyphenated_module` from
 `conftest.py` fixtures.
- Phase 6: added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (ruff + pytest on push/PR).
 Caught a real Python-version bug here: `chunking.py`'s use of
 `Path.full_match` (3.13+ only) passed locally on a 3.14 dev venv but broke
 CI's 3.12 runner — bumped `requires-python`/`ruff.toml`/CI to 3.13
 everywhere.
- Phase 7: updated `INSTALL.md`, `INSTALL-linux.md`, `COCOINDEX.md`,
 `README.md`, `METRICS.md`, `NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` for the new layout and
 console-script invocation, while explicitly noting the deployed
 `launchd`/`.cursor/mcp.json` cutover was a *planned follow-up, not yet done*.
- Merged to `main` as a clean fast-forward (CI green) rather than doing phase
 8 on the feature branch — the `~/.hindsight/*.py` symlinks that live
 services depend on point into whatever branch is checked out locally, so
 switching the local checkout to `main` *before* merging would have broken
 every live service until the PR landed.

**Phase 8 (this entry) — the cutover itself, done in 7 checkpointed stages,
each verified live before moving to the next**:

1. DCM: `dcm-nightly-ingest.sh` wrapper + `nightly-dcm.plist` → verified via a
 full ~45-minute backfill across 12 repos (2 pre-existing, unrelated
 `hindsight_retain` HTTP 422s noted, not caused by the cutover).
2. Koku: `cocoindex.koku.plist` + `nightly-koku.plist` → verified via a clean
 nightly run (triage/retain/reflect/recall probes on `claude-haiku-4-5`).
3. Praxis: same pair → verified clean, pre-existing FSEvents "already
 scheduled" warning confirmed unrelated (documented earlier this month).
4. Engram: `cocoindex.engram.plist` → verified against the branch-scoped
 mirror, confirmed synced to the just-merged `main` commit.
5. `hindsight-proxy`: the single riskiest swap (it's the *only* thing that
 ever binds port 8888 — see the 2026-08-02 blue/green entry). Reload
 completed in ~2s; confirmed with a live `curl` through the proxy to the
 `cursor-memory` MCP endpoint (200 OK), not just a port-listening check.
6. Kubernaut `nightly.plist` + `hourly.plist` → both triggered manually and
 verified end-to-end.
7. Kubernaut's live `cocoindex.service.plist` (the service from the
 2026-08-12 first-follow-up RCA above) → reloaded cleanly, all 4 apps
 (docs/code/transcripts/issues) relaunched, hindsight-api handshake OK,
 stable over a 30s observation window with issue-polling progressing
 normally.

**Two discoveries during phase 8 that changed the plan**:

1. **`io.vectorize.cocoindex.search.plist` was stale and never actually
 deployed.** There's no corresponding file under `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`
 for it. Kubernaut's search MCP server is actually spawned per-workspace by
 Cursor itself via each repo's `.cursor/mcp.json` `command`/`args` (stdio
 transport), not by `launchd` at all — the repo's plist file is vestigial.
 Updated it for consistency but there was nothing to reload.
2. **A "confirmed dead" symlink almost wasn't.** The original cleanup plan
 (5 flat shared-lib symlinks assumed unused since the phase 1-4 real-import
 migration) was wrong for one of the five: `~/.hindsight/contradiction_resolution.py`
 is a live runtime dependency of `hooks/_hindsight_check_worker.py` — the
 Deterministic Correction Enforcement hook (2026-08-04 entry) deployed via
 `hooks/install.sh` into `.cursor/hooks.json` across every onboarded repo.
 That hook script can't import `engram.contradiction_resolution` as a normal
 package member because it runs from *inside other repos'* `.cursor/hooks`
 directory with no `engram` package context at all — the flat symlink +
 `sys.path.insert(0, "~/.hindsight")` pattern is the intentional, permanent
 exception, not leftover cruft. Caught by grepping the whole repo for the
 `sys.path.insert(0, os.path.expanduser("~/.hindsight"))` pattern before
 deleting anything, rather than trusting the todo's premise. Removed only
 the 3 genuinely-dead ones (`chunking.py`, `correction_gate.py`,
 `project_scope.py`); `fallback_extract.py`'s symlink no longer existed
 either way.

**The 35-repo `.cursor/mcp.json` rewrite (14 `dcm-project` + 3 `koku` + 10
`praxis-proxy` + 8 `kubernaut`)**: spiked before executing and found the "35
files" are actually backed by only **8 physical files** — most are symlinks
into 4 shared per-family templates under `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/`
(`dcm-family-{go,nogo}.json`, `kubernaut-family-{go,nogo}.json}`, from the
2026-08-11 shared-template rollout), so editing one repo's path writes through
to every repo sharing that template. Wrote a small idempotent Python script
(dry-run first) keyed off each entry's existing `args[0]` filename prefix
(`dcm-`/`koku-`/`praxis-`/none→kubernaut) to rewrite `cocoindex-code` to
`engram-search-<project>` with empty `args`, preserving `env` (including
`KUBERNAUT_LIVE_CLONE_DIR`) and the `serena`/`hindsight-*` entries untouched.
Smoke-tested all 5 `engram-search-*` console scripts standalone (clean
"Starting ... MCP server (stdio)" + graceful exit on stdin EOF) before
applying broadly. Verified all 35 logical paths post-apply by resolving
symlinks and checking the live command, not just the symlink targets.
Also caught two things outside the "35 external repos" frame that would
otherwise have been missed: engram's **own** `.cursor/mcp.json` (still on the
flat symlink, since it's not one of the "external" repos) and an orphaned,
currently-unreferenced `kubernaut-family-nogo.json` template — fixed both for
consistency even though nothing currently depends on the latter.

**Final symlink cleanup**: removed all 13 now-dead flat `~/.hindsight/*.py`
symlinks (5 `*-cocoindex-search.py`, 5 `*-cocoindex-flows.py`,
`hindsight-proxy.py`, `ingest-issues.py`, `nightly-learn.py`) only after
confirming zero remaining references in any deployed plist or `.cursor/mcp.json`
— the handful of hits from a broad grep were all stale comments/docstrings
mentioning historical filenames, not live imports. `contradiction_resolution.py`
and the unrelated `prefilter-shadow-trial.py` spike symlink are the only two
left. Also unloaded and removed the already-deprecated
`io.vectorize.hindsight.issues.plist` (superseded by CocoIndex's 5-minute
issue polling, confirmed via its own header comment and `docs/INSTALL.md`'s
pre-existing phase-8 cleanup instructions).

**Process note**: executed stage-by-stage with an explicit checkpoint after
each one (user approval to proceed), and — per user instruction — ran a
preflight/spike before every remaining cleanup task with an explicit <90%
confidence escalation gate. That gate is what caught the
`contradiction_resolution.py` near-miss instead of it shipping as a silent
regression discovered only when some other repo's correction-enforcement hook
started failing open.

## 2026-08-12 (same day, fourth follow-up): Host at 100% CPU — `hindsight-api` Grinding a Sonnet-Tier Consolidation Backlog, Compounded by 23 of 45 Mental Models Silently Running Full Re-Synthesis Instead of Delta

**Trigger**: user reported "host is 100% cpu" mid-session, unrelated to the
day's other work. RCA requested.

**Findings**: `hindsight-api` (PID 78703 at the time) was pinned at 220%+
CPU, system load average 8.55/7.85/10.20. Cause was a mix of scale and
misconfiguration, not a single bug:

- **Consolidation backlog, uncapped growth**: `kubernaut-docs` had ~8,500
 unconsolidated memories (chronic, over a week old), `praxis-docs` had
 ~2,400 and *growing* (1,151 → 2,400 in about an hour) — Praxis's newly
 enabled live ingestion (see the automation entry above) was outpacing its
 consolidation throughput. `HINDSIGHT_API_WORKER_MAX_SLOTS=5` lets up to 5
 banks consolidate concurrently, each making sequential LLM calls against
 Vertex AI on `vertex_ai/claude-sonnet-4-6` ($3/$15 per Mtok), 25-35s per
 call.
- **Slow dedup-adjudication queries compounding it**: Postgres
 `pg_stat_activity` showed `pgvector`-backed `SELECT`s for consolidation's
 near-duplicate adjudication running 34+ minutes, some 2+ hours, frequently
 hitting `asyncpg.TimeoutError` (304 recorded) — CPU was going partly to
 genuine LLM-bound work and partly to Postgres churning on these slow
 queries.
- **The real "why can't we just consume the delta" bug**: querying
 `mental_models.trigger->>'mode'` directly found **23 of 45** mental models
 across *every* project bank — all 5 `kubernaut-docs`, both
 `kubernaut-issues`, all 3 `praxis-docs`, both `dcm-issues`, all 3
 `dcm-docs`, both `koku-docs`, both `engram-docs`, plus 3 stray
 `cursor-memory` ones — had `trigger.mode` unset (defaults to `"full"`)
 instead of `"delta"`. Per `hindsight_api`'s
 `MemoryEngine.refresh_mental_model()`, `mode="full"` re-runs the *entire*
 `reflect()` synthesis over every fact matching the model's `source_query`
 on **every** refresh; `mode="delta"` does a cheap surgical edit merging
 only what changed since `last_refreshed_source_query`. With backlogs this
 size and refreshes firing on every matching new fact, full-mode models
 were re-synthesizing from thousands of facts repeatedly instead of
 incrementally — the dominant, avoidable cost driver, confirmed after the
 fix by `refresh_mental_model` task claims dropping from a constant stream
 (multiple per poller tick) to zero over a 3+ minute monitoring window.

**Fix** (four changes, none touch application code — all `~/.hindsight/config.env` or direct SQL against `hindsight-api`'s own tables, so none show up in this repo's diff):

1. Stop-the-bleeding kill switch: `HINDSIGHT_API_WORKER_MAX_SLOTS=0`.
 Verified `HINDSIGHT_API_ENABLE_AUTO_CONSOLIDATION=false` alone is
 **insufficient** — it only stops *new* consolidation ops from being
 enqueued after retain, not the worker pool draining an already-queued
 backlog (3 consolidation ops kept getting claimed/run after setting it and
 restarting). `WORKER_MAX_SLOTS=0` is the real switch: it disables the
 whole background worker pool (consolidation, `refresh_mental_model`,
 `graph_maintenance`, `import_documents`) while leaving live
 ingestion untouched, since `retain`/`recall` are called synchronously
 in-process (`mcp_tools.retain` → `memory_engine.retain`), never through
 this worker queue. Gotcha hit along the way: setting `WORKER_MAX_SLOTS=0`
 without also zeroing `HINDSIGHT_API_WORKER_CONSOLIDATION_MAX_SLOTS`
 (default reservation of 2) fails `hindsight-api`'s config validation at
 startup (`Sum of per-operation slot reservations (2) exceeds
 worker_max_slots (0)`) and crash-loops the service — both must be set
 together.
2. Cheaper model: `HINDSIGHT_API_LLM_MODEL` switched from
 `vertex_ai/claude-sonnet-4-6` ($3/$15 per Mtok) to
 `vertex_ai/claude-haiku-4-5@20251001` ($1/$5 per Mtok, confirmed via
 Anthropic's published pricing as the cheapest current non-retired Haiku —
 Haiku 4 is retired, Haiku 3.5 is being phased out except on
 Bedrock/Vertex) — the same model already proven working in this exact
 deployment via `HINDSIGHT_API_RETAIN_LLM_MODEL`, so zero new
 provider/auth risk.
3. Fixed the actual delta bug: `UPDATE mental_models SET trigger =
 jsonb_set(trigger, '{mode}', '"delta"') WHERE
 COALESCE(trigger->>'mode','full') = 'full'` — 23 rows updated, 0 remain in
 `full` mode.
4. Re-enabled the worker pool (`WORKER_MAX_SLOTS=5`) and actively monitored
 for several minutes: confirmed 100% of consolidation LLM calls now show
 `model=litellm/vertex_ai/claude-haiku-4-5@20251001` (zero stray Sonnet
 calls), zero `refresh_mental_model` claims, zero new errors/timeouts,
 `hindsight-api` CPU down to single digits (7-19%, from 220%+), and
 `koku-issues` (3/3) and `praxis-issues` (12/12) backlogs fully drained
 within the first two minutes at ~2-6s/memory (down from ~13-25s/memory on
 Sonnet).

**Not fixed / left as-is**: the underlying Postgres slow-query root cause
for `pg_stat_activity`'s 34min-2hr adjudication queries and the 304
recorded `TimeoutError`s — the cheaper model and delta-mode fix removed the
cost pressure that made this urgent, but the queries themselves may still
warrant an index/query review if `pg_stat_activity` shows them recurring
under the new, lighter load.

---

## 2026-08-12: RCA — Kubernaut's Live CocoIndex Ingestion Was Being Knocked Offline Every Night for 9 Nights Straight by a "One-Time" 2026-08-03 Cleanup Job That Never Successfully Self-Removed

**Trigger**: routine "status update from yesterday's run" check found
`io.vectorize.cocoindex.service` (kubernaut's live ingestion) completely
absent from `launchctl list` — not crash-looping, fully unloaded from the
`gui/$UID` domain. Initial hypothesis (a recurring `resource_tracker: leaked
semaphore` warning right before the process went silent, at `02:56` on
2026-08-11 and `02:59:53` on 2026-08-12) turned out to be a **red herring**:
that warning fires from the embedding step's `loky` worker-pool recycling
and is unrelated to the actual cause — it just happens to land near the
crash time by coincidence, since the real culprit runs on its own fixed
3:00 AM schedule.

**Actual root cause, found via `/usr/bin/log show` (note: plain `log` is a
zsh builtin on this machine and silently no-ops — must invoke the full path)
against the exact outage window**:

```
03:00:05.319 launchd: [.../io.vectorize.cocoindex.cleanup-once] Successfully spawned cleanup-kubernaut-docs-issues.sh
03:00:05.595 launchd: [.../io.vectorize.cocoindex.service [37423]] bootout initiated by: launchctl[69706]<-bash[69698]
03:00:05.595 launchd: [.../io.vectorize.cocoindex.service [37423]] signaled service: Terminated: 15
```

`io.vectorize.cocoindex.cleanup-once.plist` was a **one-time** 2026-08-03
job (see that date's entry) meant to fire once at 3:00 AM, stop the live
service, clear the bloated `kubernaut-docs`/`kubernaut-issues` banks, run a
fresh backfill, restart the live service, then self-delete its own plist so
it would never fire again. Its own log
(`~/.hindsight/logs/kubernaut-docs-issues-cleanup.log`) shows it has instead
fired **every single night since 2026-08-04** (9 consecutive nights) and
failed at the *same* step every time except once:

```
2026-08-04 03:01:06 - DELETE kubernaut-docs/memories -> HTTP 500: {"detail":"Failed to delete agent data: "}
2026-08-05 03:00:04 - ERROR: hindsight-api not healthy -- aborting, nothing touched
2026-08-06 03:01:09 - DELETE kubernaut-docs/memories -> HTTP 500: {"detail":"Failed to delete agent data: "}
2026-08-07 .. 2026-08-12 - same HTTP 500, every night
```

The script (correctly, defensively) `exit 1`s the instant that `DELETE
/v1/default/banks/kubernaut-docs/memories` call returns non-200, **before**
ever reaching the "restart `io.vectorize.cocoindex.service`" line or the
self-removal block at the bottom. Net effect: every night at 3:00 AM sharp,
kubernaut's live ingestion gets stopped and then **never restarted**,
staying down until someone happens to notice and manually
`launchctl bootstrap`s it — which is exactly what happened on 2026-08-11
(caught ~6h later, misattributed at the time to plist env-var drift) and
2026-08-12 (caught ~8.5h later, this investigation). Nights in between
(08-06 through 08-10) likely had the same outage but went uncaught/
unnoticed, or self-resolved on their own timescale — no one was checking
`launchctl list` for this specific job daily.

The `HTTP 500: {"detail":"Failed to delete agent data: "}` itself is an
opaque hindsight-api-side error (empty exception message — some caught
exception's `str(e)` is empty) with no matching traceback found in
`hindsight-stderr-{blue,green}.log` for the request window (those logs
carry no timestamps, making precise correlation impossible from the client
side); hindsight-api's source isn't in this repo/checkout, so the exact
handler bug needs investigation from whichever repo owns it. Given the
job's original purpose — a one-time historical dedup of banks that had
ballooned to ~3.5-4x redundancy from now-fixed feature-branch churn — is
already stale (nightly `triage()` now runs its own ongoing near-duplicate/
consolidation pass, confirmed via the `2026-08-12.json` nightly log showing
routine `near-duplicate`/`repeated-fact` flagging), re-debugging the delete
endpoint just to let this one-time job finally succeed isn't worth it.

**Fix**: unloaded and deleted the stale
`io.vectorize.cocoindex.cleanup-once.plist` and its `ProgramArguments`
target script — the exact self-removal the script itself would have done on
a successful run, just performed manually 9 days late. This directly
eliminates the nightly 3 AM outage; kubernaut's live service no longer has
anything intentionally stopping it in the small hours.

**Instrumentation added** (the ask that motivated digging past the
red-herring warning): a lesson here worth generalizing — this script's bug
class (stop-a-long-running-service, do-fallible-work, restart-at-the-bottom
of a linear script) will recur any time a similar maintenance job is
written, because any early `exit`/failure path skips the restart. Two
changes:
1. **Any future "stop service → do work → restart service" script must use
 `trap 'launchctl bootstrap ...' EXIT` (or equivalent) so the restart runs
 on *every* exit path, not just the happy one at the bottom** — noted as a
 project convention going forward, not just for this one script.
2. **A generic `launchd` watchdog**, independent of root cause, as a safety
 net for this and any future silent-outage class: see the follow-up entry
 (2026-08-12, watchdog) for the plist added.

**Ruled out** (from the earlier, now-superseded theory): not memory
pressure/jetsam (no matching kernel log entries), not a sleep/wake event (no
`pmset -g log` hits in the window), not the same-day flows/search reorg
(that reorg's restart at 16:39 on 2026-08-11 ran clean for 10+ hours before
this unrelated 3 AM job fired), and not a recurrence of the 2026-08-11 plist
env-var drift fix (still intact in the deployed plist).

**Debugging note**: `log` is a zsh shell builtin on this machine (silently
no-ops instead of erroring, `(eval):log:3: too many arguments`) — the
unified log must be queried via the full path `/usr/bin/log show`, not bare
`log show`, or you'll get zero results and wrongly conclude there's nothing
in the window.
