## 2026-08-11 (same day, fourth follow-up): "MCPs Disabled Across the Board" Traced to Two Separate Things — a Confirmed Cursor UI Bug (Not a Real Disable) and a Real, Now-Closed `git reset`/`git rebase` Gap in the Serena/`gopls` Self-Healing Hooks

**Trigger**: user reported seeing MCP servers shown as disabled across every open
Cursor window and asked whether switching branches caused it, or whether the
servers were actually down (having already restarted things manually) — then,
on follow-up, clarified it wasn't just `hindsight`-family (`http`-transport)
servers but `cocoindex-code` and Serena (`stdio`-transport) too.

**Finding #1 — branch switching / a real backend outage both ruled out for the
`hindsight`-family symptom.** `hindsight-api`'s active ("green") instance and the
`hindsight-proxy` in front of it had both been running continuously since the
prior night's 22:16 restart, with zero sleep/wake cycles on the machine in that
window (`pmset -g log`) and a clean `HTTP 200` health check. The one real backend
hiccup overnight — the 01:00 blue/green swap failing to bootstrap the *standby*
— never got far enough to flip the proxy, so it shouldn't have touched the
actively-serving connection either. This repo's own 2026-07-31 finding
(`docs/findings/2026-07.md`) already documents that Cursor's `http`-type MCP
client does not auto-retry after any dropped connection to a local server — a
momentary blip on that one shared `localhost:8888` connection, used by nearly
every currently-open project window, would explain an "across the board"
appearance from a single shared dependency without it being a Cursor-wide bug or
a branch switch.

**Finding #2 — direct proof the "Disabled" label was largely fiction.** Queried
`cursor/disabledMcpServers` directly out of each affected workspace's
`~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/User/workspaceStorage/<hash>/state.vscdb`
(the actual persisted source of truth Cursor's Settings UI is supposed to
reflect — flagged as a known divergence point in `post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh`'s
own header comment, added when that script was changed to avoid unnecessarily
re-touching `.cursor/mcp.json` on every checkout for exactly this reason).
Result: `kubernaut`, `kubernaut-console`, `kubernaut-v1.5`, `kubernaut-v1.6`,
`engram`, and `osac-service-provider` all showed `[]` — genuinely, persistently
**empty** — meaning none of those servers are actually disabled in stored state;
the red/"Disabled" label the user was seeing in the UI for those windows is a
stale rendering, not a real toggle. (`koku` was the one real exception:
`["user-hindsight","user-gopls"]` — a real, persisted disable, presumably from
an earlier manual toggle, not a UI glitch — left as-is, flagged for the user
rather than silently re-enabled.) Practical implication: for the false-positive
windows, a **window reload** clears the stale label immediately since there's
no real state to fix; a git-hook-side fix cannot touch this UI-only bug at all.

**Finding #3 — a real, separate, and now-closed gap: `serena`/`gopls` intentionally
get killed by this repo's own self-healing git hooks on checkout/merge (see the
2026-08-08 finding above), but `git reset` and `git rebase` don't fire either
`post-checkout` or `post-merge`**, so a repo whose last operation was one of those
(confirmed live: `kubernaut-console`'s reflog showed `reset: moving to
origin/main` as its most recent entry) has a Serena/`gopls` process that may be
stale or simply not running, with nothing having refreshed it. This is exactly
the same underlying issue as the 2026-08-08 entry's "known gap, not closed" note
about `git rebase` — reset turned out to hit the identical blind spot.

**Fix**: added a new shared hook, `reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`, using
Git's `reference-transaction` hook — the one hook that fires for *any*
ref-updating command, not just checkout/merge. Confirmed empirically in a
scratch repo (not a real checkout) that both `git reset --hard` and `git
rebase` drive a reference transaction that updates `refs/heads/<branch>` (or
literally `HEAD` when detached), so filtering on "did this transaction touch
the ref I'm currently on" and calling the existing `_restart-gopls.sh`/
`_restart-serena.sh` helpers on a match closes the reset gap *and* the
already-flagged rebase gap in one hook, without needing a dedicated hook for
each. Deliberately does **not** touch `.cursor/mcp.json` (unlike
`post-checkout.sh`) — re-touching that file on every ref update (this hook
fires far more often than checkout/merge, e.g. on background fetches) would
multiply exposure to the Cursor MCP-client reload-on-config-change bug from
Finding #2 rather than the intended process-kill-only side effect. Always
exits 0 regardless of outcome — a non-zero exit during a transaction's
"preparing"/"prepared" states would abort it, which must never happen here.

**Verification, without touching a real live process**: end-to-end test using a
harmless fake process (`sleep 300` renamed to look like `gopls mcp`, `cwd` set to
`kubernaut-console`'s toplevel) confirmed it survives a simulated non-HEAD ref
update (e.g. a remote-tracking ref from a background fetch) and is correctly
killed by a simulated HEAD/current-branch ref update — and that a real,
unrelated `gopls mcp` process for a different, non-kubernaut-family repo was
left untouched throughout. Self-provisioning wired into `post-checkout-cursor-
mcp.sh` the same way `post-merge` already self-provisions, and backfilled the
symlink into all 5 kubernaut-family repos immediately (matching the 2026-08-08
precedent) rather than waiting for their next checkout — verified each repo's
*actual* `core.hooksPath` individually rather than assuming `.git/hooks`, the
exact assumption-bug the 2026-08-08 entry caught for `kubernaut-operator`.

**Takeaway**: "MCP shows disabled" is not one bug — in this session it was two
unrelated things wearing the same symptom (a Cursor UI staleness bug with no
real fix available from this repo, and a genuine self-inflicted process-kill gap
that was fixable). Treating every occurrence as the same root cause would have
missed the actual fix; checking the persisted `disabledMcpServers` state
directly was what separated "real" from "cosmetic" here.

---

## 2026-08-11 (same day, fifth follow-up): `osac-service-provider` Was Missing Serena — Turned Out None of the 14 `dcm-project` Repos Had It; Rolled Out the Full Shared-Template + Self-Healing-Hook Pattern to All of Them

**Trigger**: user reported "serena is missing in the dcm project:
osac-service-provider is lacking it."

**Finding — not an isolated gap.** Checked `osac-service-provider`'s
`.cursor/mcp.json` first: confirmed it had `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`/
`cocoindex-code` but no `serena` entry. Widened the check to all 14
`dcm-project` repos on disk and found **zero** had `serena` — this wasn't
drift in one repo, it was a component of the 2026-08-10 Serena rollout
(documented above and in `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` step 7) that simply
never reached `dcm-project` at all. Root cause of *why* it never reached them:
unlike kubernaut-family (`kubernaut`/`-v1.5`/`-v1.6`/`-operator`, all four
sharing one `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/kubernaut-family-go.json` via
symlink) or the praxis-proxy org (same pattern, Rust variant), every
`dcm-project` repo's `.cursor/mcp.json` was an independent, real
(non-symlink) file — so dcm had no shared template and no self-healing
`post-checkout`/`post-merge`/`reference-transaction` git hooks to retrofit in
the first place. All 14 real files were byte-identical to each other, so this
was a from-scratch rollout, not a merge of divergent configs. (`osac-demo-recording`,
also under `~/go/src/github.com/dcm-project/`, has no `.git` at all — a demo
asset/video directory, not a real clone — excluded throughout.)

Asked the user to confirm scope before touching 14 repos: **rolled out to all
14**, adopting the **same shared-template-symlink + self-healing-hook
architecture as kubernaut-family** (not just hand-editing each repo's real
file in place).

**What was built**:
- `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/dcm-family-go.json` /
 `dcm-family-nogo.json` — same 4-server shape as the existing dcm mcp.json
 (`hindsight-docs`→`dcm-docs`, `hindsight-issues`→`dcm-issues`,
 `cocoindex-code`→`dcm-cocoindex-search.py`) plus a `serena` entry identical
 in form to kubernaut-family's (generic `uvx ... serena start-mcp-server
 --project ${workspaceFolder} ...` invocation — nothing kubernaut-specific
 about it). As with kubernaut-family today, go/nogo content ended up
 identical except JSON array formatting; kept both files anyway so the
 hook's per-repo `go.mod`-presence branch stays meaningful if the two ever
 need to diverge.
- `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/post-checkout-dcm-mcp.sh` — a **separate** script
 from `post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh`, not a generalization of it. Editing the
 shared kubernaut-family script to branch on repo family was considered and
 rejected: that script is live for 5 kubernaut-family + praxis-proxy repos
 today, and the 2026-08-11 (earlier) correction on record — "changes should
 apply only to DCM repos, not kubernaut repos" — argued for zero shared-file
 risk over less duplication. The two scripts differ only in which template
 filenames they select; both reuse the exact same family-agnostic
 `post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh`, `reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`,
 `_restart-gopls.sh`, and `_restart-serena.sh` helpers (none of those four
 contain anything kubernaut-specific — they operate purely on `$TOPLEVEL`),
 so no duplication was needed there.
- Applied to all 14 repos: converted each real `.cursor/mcp.json` into a
 symlink (8 repos with a root `go.mod` → `dcm-family-go.json`; the other 6 →
 `dcm-family-nogo.json`), installed `post-checkout`/`post-merge`/
 `reference-transaction` hook symlinks (all 14 already used plain
 `.git/hooks`, no custom `core.hooksPath` to trip over this time), and ran
 the restart helpers once rather than waiting for each repo's next checkout.
- Per-repo `.serena/project.yml` (can't be shared/symlinked — Serena keys
 project registration by absolute path, per `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` step
 7's explicit gotcha): `go` for the 8 `go.mod` repos plus `utilities` (whose
 only `go.mod` is nested at `tests/e2e/go.mod`, not root — the hook's own
 root-only `go.mod` check classifies it as "nogo" for template purposes,
 which doesn't matter content-wise since go/nogo are identical, but its
 `.serena/project.yml` was still set to `go` on its actual code composition:
 26 `.go` files, the most substantial content in the repo); `markdown` for
 `dcm` and `enhancements` (docs/taxonomy and enhancement-proposal repos,
 15–3 `.md` files respectively, no code); `ansible` for `quadlet-deploy`
 (`roles/`/`molecule/`/`*_task.yml` — a real Ansible role, not generic YAML);
 `bash` for `shared-workflows` (`hack/*.sh` + one GH workflow). `udlm`
 skipped entirely — genuinely empty (only Cursor-agent scaffolding, no
 source of any kind) — got the shared `mcp.json` for architectural
 consistency but no `.serena/project.yml`, since there's nothing to
 register.
- `.gitignore`: added the same `.cursor/*` / `!.cursor/rules/` /
 `!.cursor/skills/` block (12 of 13 real repos lacked any `.cursor` entry at
 all; `osac-service-provider` already had it from its own prior setup) plus
 a `.serena/` entry with the standard "trial, not yet a team decision"
 wording, to all 13 real repos (all had zero `.serena` entry beforehand).

**Verification — spot-checked `serena project health-check` rather than all
14** (bounded time, matching the 2026-08-10 praxis-proxy precedent of
spot-checking rather than exhaustively checking every repo in a same-language
batch):
- `osac-service-provider` (go, the repo the user actually flagged): **passed**
 — `gopls` started in ~0.2s, `GetSymbolsOverviewTool`/`FindSymbolTool`/
 `FindReferencingSymbolsTool` all worked against real code (found 3 real
 references for a real function).
- `shared-workflows` (bash — no prior track record for this language in this
 environment): **passed** — bash-language-server started (~7s), correctly
 extracted real function/variable symbols from `hack/tag-release.sh` and
 found a real reference.
- `enhancements` (markdown — also no prior track record): **passed** —
 treated a real markdown heading as a findable/referenceable symbol.
- `quadlet-deploy` (ansible — also no prior track record): **the CLI
 health-check failed**, but only because its own file-picker landed on a
 root-level `verify_compose_alignment.yml` the `ansible` language server
 doesn't recognize as analyzable. Re-tested directly against a real,
 correctly-located Ansible file (`roles/dcm_deploy/tasks/main.yml`) via
 Serena's Python API (same technique the 2026-08-10 plan used to route
 around this exact CLI file-picker weakness) — no crash, but
 `get_symbol_overview` returned `[]`: Serena's `ansible` language server
 integration doesn't error on real Ansible content, it just doesn't surface
 task/role structure as "symbols" at all. **Left as `ansible` anyway**
 (correctly matches the actual content — a generic `yaml` server would have
 no more claim to being "right" here and wasn't independently verified to do
 better): a real but disclosed, non-blocking limitation, the same category as
 Go's Ginkgo-block and TypeScript's cross-package-via-build-output
 limitations documented in the 2026-08-10 rollout — Serena's other 51 tools
 (file read/write/search, shell execution, memories, etc.) are unaffected
 either way.

**Takeaway**: a single-repo bug report ("X is missing Y") is worth checking
against siblings before fixing narrowly — here the real gap was an entire
project's onboarding step that got skipped, not one repo's drift from a
correctly-onboarded baseline. Kept the fix strictly dcm-scoped (new,
separate hook script and templates) rather than generalizing the working
kubernaut-family script, consistent with the standing "DCM-only changes"
correction from earlier today.

## 2026-08-13: Cursor's "hindsight" MCP Server Shown as Errored Was Reported as a `praxis-grid` Issue — Traced to a Chronic Background-Job Timeout on the Shared `hindsight-api` Backend, Plus a Nightly Blue/Green Restart That Silently No-Ops ~Half the Time

**Trigger**: user shared a screenshot of Cursor's "Needs Attention" panel
showing the `hindsight` MCP server in an error state, asking to "triage and
address this issue in the praxis-grid repo."

**Scope correction, first**: `praxis-grid`'s own `.cursor/mcp.json` doesn't
define a plain `hindsight` server at all — only `hindsight-docs`/
`hindsight-issues`, both pointed at the same shared backend as every other
repo. The errored `hindsight` entry comes from the **global**
`~/.cursor/mcp.json` (`http://localhost:8888/mcp/cursor-memory/`). This
would surface identically in any repo; there was nothing `praxis-grid`-
specific to fix there.

**Root cause A (the actual intermittent-error driver)**: the active
`hindsight-api` backend process (blue, up ~31h at the time of triage) had a
background graph-maintenance query (`prune_stale_cooccurrences` in the
pip-installed `hindsight_api` package, v0.8.5) failing with Postgres
`TimeoutError` **359 times** in that window — roughly once every 5 minutes,
continuously retrying and failing (`~/.hindsight/logs/hindsight-stderr-
blue.log`). This is the same query already restructured once for a
documented prior incident (its own code comments reference issues #2529/
#2473 — an INTERSECT-based staleness check replacing a self-join that hit
28-30min per sweep on a high-degree hub entity), but it's still timing out
at current data scale. Because it shares the small
`HINDSIGHT_API_DB_POOL_MAX_SIZE=15` connection pool with real MCP request
traffic, sustained failures here plausibly explain the "hindsight not
connected" / `ConnectionRefusedError` symptom reported intermittently across
many past sessions (not a one-off). `hindsight_api` is a pip-installed
package, not source under our control — no direct code fix available here;
flagging for upstream/config-level follow-up (no `GRAPH_MAINTENANCE_*` or
statement-timeout env knob currently exists in `~/.hindsight/config.env` to
tune this without a code change).

**Root cause B (compounding factor, and the one actually fixed here)**: the
nightly recycle that exists specifically to reset root cause A
(`~/.hindsight/hindsight-blue-green-restart.sh`, tracked in this repo, run
via `io.vectorize.hindsight.restart.plist` at 01:00 daily) failed 4 of the
last 8 nights (2026-08-05, 08-06, 08-11, 08-13) with `launchctl bootstrap:
Bootstrap failed: 5: Input/output error` when trying to bring up the standby
color — almost certainly because 1am is exactly when the Mac is most likely
asleep/locked and the `gui/<uid>` Aqua session domain isn't fully available
to accept a new bootstrap. The call was unguarded: `set -euo pipefail` killed
the whole script the instant it failed, with no retry and no log entry
beyond "swap start" (confirmed by gaps in `blue-green-restart.log` on exactly
those 4 dates). Net effect: the heap-fragmentation/backlog-clearing restart
silently no-ops on roughly half of nights, letting the same backend process
(and its accumulating timeout backlog) run far longer than the 24h the design
intends.

**Fix**: added `bootstrap_with_retry()` to
`hindsight-blue-green-restart.sh` — 3 attempts with backoff (5s, 10s) around
the `launchctl bootstrap` call, logging each failed attempt's captured
stderr before giving up (previously that stderr only reached the plist's own
`restart-stderr.log`, never `blue-green-restart.log`, making this pattern
hard to spot without cross-referencing two log files). Smoke-tested the
retry function in isolation (faked `launchctl` to fail twice then succeed →
confirmed 3-attempt success path; faked it to always fail → confirmed clean
exhaustion after exactly 3 attempts, no infinite loop). Then ran the real
script live (mid-morning, GUI session active) to force an out-of-schedule
swap and clear the current backend's 359-error backlog immediately — bootstrap
succeeded on the first attempt as expected outside the 1am window; new
`green` instance passed health checks and took over in 24s.

**Verified via direct protocol probe, not just `/health`**: a raw MCP
`initialize` JSON-RPC POST to `http://localhost:8888/mcp/cursor-memory/`
returned a valid `200` response post-swap, confirming the proxy/backend path
itself is healthy end-to-end. `GetMcpTools` in this session still reported
the `user-hindsight` server as errored afterward — that's Cursor's own
cached client-side connection state from before the swap, not a live
problem; it needs a manual reconnect (toggle the server off/on in Cursor
Settings → MCP, or restart Cursor) to clear, which isn't triggerable from
an agent session.

**Not addressed** (out of scope for this fix, flagged for follow-up): the
chronic `prune_stale_cooccurrences` timeout itself (needs either an
upstream `hindsight_api` fix, a DB-side investigation of why the existing
index-only-scan optimization no longer bounds cost at current data scale, or
a config knob that doesn't yet exist); `~/.hindsight/logs/` has grown to
2.0GB unrotated (not urgent today — 235GB free — but no rotation policy
exists).

**Takeaway**: an error surfaced "in repo X" isn't necessarily caused by
anything in repo X — check whether the failing server/tool is even defined
at the project level before assuming project-level scope. Also: a
self-healing mechanism (blue/green restart) that itself silently fails
needs its own failure visibility, not just a retry — the gap here wasn't
that swaps failed sometimes, it was that failures were invisible without
manually diffing two separate log files.

**Follow-up (same day): the chronic `prune_stale_cooccurrences` fix is
already merged upstream — but not yet released**. User recalled having
filed an upstream issue about this exact query. Found it:
`hindsight-api`/`hindsight-api-slim` is a real PyPI package published from
[vectorize-io/hindsight](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight) (we
maintain a fork, `jordigilh/hindsight`, at
`~/go/src/github.com/jordigilh/hindsight`). Issue
[#3367](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight/issues/3367) — filed by
`jordigilh`, describing this exact scaling pattern ("scales poorly with
hub-entity degree, 88-140s on a real bank") — was closed 2026-08-12 by PR
[#3408](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight/pull/3408)
("perf(graph): decouple stale-cooccurrence prune from hub-entity degree"),
which replaces the per-row correlated `INTERSECT` with a `live` CTE
(entity-pair membership) built once per sweep — their own reproduction
(12,000-degree hub, 14,000 cooccurrence rows) measured **215,329ms →
~250ms**. A second, related merge landed the same week:
[#3451](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight/pull/3451) (merged
2026-08-13, same morning), a set-wise rewrite of `expand_observations`'
per-candidate scoring for issue #3085 — jordigilh contributed to that
thread too (ruling out prepared-statement plan caching, and finding #3367
"along the way").

**Both fixes are unreleased.** Latest PyPI/GitHub release is `v0.9.0`
(tagged 2026-08-07) — 5 days *before* #3408 merged. Installed version here
is `0.8.5`. Checked all published versions
(`0.0.13`…`0.9.0`, 60 releases) via the PyPI JSON API to confirm `0.9.0`
really is latest, not a lookup gap.

**Decision**: wait for the next official `hindsight-api` PyPI release
rather than installing from the unreleased `main` branch or hand-backporting
just the #3408 SQL change into the installed `site-packages`. No code
changes made as part of this follow-up. **Action item**: when a new release
ships (check `curl -s https://pypi.org/pypi/hindsight-api/json | python3 -c
"import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['info']['version'])"`, or `gh
release list --repo vectorize-io/hindsight --limit 1`), upgrade
`~/.hindsight/venv`'s `hindsight-api`/`hindsight-api-slim` and confirm via
the same `hindsight-stderr-{blue,green}.log` grep for
`prune_stale_cooccurrences` TimeoutErrors that the chronic timeout is
actually gone post-upgrade, not just assumed fixed from the changelog.

---
## 2026-08-16: Root-Caused and Fixed the kubernaut-family Daemon Restart Storm (CocoIndex/Serena Intermittently Failing on kubernaut-v1.5)

**Trigger**: user reported CocoIndex and Serena failing for `kubernaut-v1.5`
with errors, recovering when logging out and back into Cursor.

**Investigation**: `serena-multiplex-kubernaut-family-stderr.log` and
`cocoindex-code-kubernaut-family-stderr.log` showed a pattern of rapid,
repeated process restarts (every few seconds to minutes) with no internal
exceptions — clean `Starting` → `Shutting down` → `Application shutdown
complete` cycles, not crashes. `serena-multiplex-kubernaut-family-stderr.log`
additionally showed `httpx.RemoteProtocolError: peer closed connection
without sending complete message body` when relaying to the upstream serena
daemon mid-restart. This matches the user's "recovers on relogin" report
exactly: reconnecting just means landing in a lucky window between kicks,
not an actual fix.

**Root cause**: `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/_restart-kubernaut-family-daemons.sh`,
invoked by `post-checkout`/`post-merge`/`reference-transaction` hooks
installed in *all 6* kubernaut-family repos, was unconditionally
`launchctl kickstart -k`-ing all 4 shared daemons (`cocoindex-code`, `serena`,
`serena-project-server`, `serena-multiplex`) on every git ref-changing
operation in any of them — including background fetches driving
`reference-transaction` for the read-only `kubernaut-v1.5`/`kubernaut-v1.6`
mirror refreshes, which fire far more often than interactive checkouts. This
also retroactively explains the `cocoindex-code` self-SIGTERM restart loop
found and only partially explained on 2026-08-15 (378 restarts in ~19 hours,
no exceptions logged) — it wasn't self-terminating, it was being force-killed
by this hook.

**Preflight/spike before changing a script shared across 6 repos**: read all
4 daemon plists and re-examined the 2026-08-13 "seventh/eighth/ninth
follow-up" entries below establishing this daemon architecture. Findings that
reshaped the fix from the user's initial "restart only if unresponsive,
watchdog-style" framing:

- All 4 daemon plists already set `KeepAlive: SuccessfulExit=false` —
  launchd already restarts any of them automatically on a real crash. A
  liveness-only watchdog would duplicate that, and still wouldn't catch the
  actual failure mode this hook exists for: a live, responsive daemon
  silently serving stale pre-checkout data.
- `cocoindex-code` answers queries against the pgvector table populated by
  the separate CocoIndex ingestion pipeline, not live checkout files — it
  never had per-checkout staleness risk.
- `serena-multiplex` is a stateless HTTP relay (+ one `asyncio.Lock`) in
  front of the real serena daemon — it caches no file state of its own.
- `serena` (port 8892) has exactly one shared "active project" LSP state
  across all 6 repos (a documented race — see the ninth-follow-up entry
  below) — the one daemon with genuine per-checkout staleness risk.
- `serena-project-server` lazily spawns one LSP subprocess per *queried*
  project (`query_project`) and can equally accumulate stale per-project
  caches.

**Fix**: rewrote `_restart-kubernaut-family-daemons.sh` to (1) drop
`cocoindex-code` and `serena-multiplex` from the restart path entirely — no
staleness risk, no downside — and (2) debounce the remaining two (`serena`,
`serena-project-server`) via a `~/.hindsight/.serena-family-restart-debounce`
timestamp file: skip the kickstart if one already fired within the last 30s,
so a burst of ref updates (a rebase, near-simultaneous hooks across repos,
mirror-refresh fetches) collapses into one restart instead of N. First
checkout after being idle still restarts immediately — the debounce only
skips *redundant* kicks. Updated the three caller hooks'
(`post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh`, `post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh`,
`reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`) header comments to match the new
scope instead of the stale "restarts the two/four shared daemons" wording.

**Verification**: ran the rewritten script directly — first invocation wrote
the debounce timestamp and kicked `serena`+`serena-project-server` (confirmed
via fresh PIDs in `launchctl print`); an immediate second invocation left the
debounce file and both daemons' PIDs unchanged (no-op, as intended). Confirmed
`cocoindex-code` and `serena-multiplex` PIDs were untouched by either run.
`sh -n` syntax-checked clean. All 4 daemons confirmed `state = running`
post-test.

**Follow-up, same day: found and fixed the same bug class affecting 30 more
repos, and shipped a proper templates directory.** User asked whether engram
ships a template for these git hooks and examples for other users deploying
engram — it didn't: the actual scripts lived only at
`~/.hindsight/git-hooks/` (not even a git repo), with `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md`
step 14 documenting install commands that assumed the source scripts already
existed.

While building `git-hooks/generic/` (single-repo variant) and `git-hooks/family/`
(`.sh.tmpl` templates + `generate-hooks.sh`, generating from a `.vars` file the
same way `cursor/generate-mdc.sh` does for Cursor rules) to close that gap,
re-reading `post-checkout-generic-mcp.sh`/`post-checkout-dcm-mcp.sh` surfaced a
second, larger bug: both self-provisioned `post-merge`/`reference-transaction`
from the kubernaut-family-*specific* scripts (`post-merge-cursor-mcp.sh` /
`reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh`) — comments claiming these were "100%
family-agnostic" were true when written, but both were rewritten 2026-08-13
to restart the kubernaut-family shared serena daemon specifically, and the
generic/dcm callers were never updated to match.

Audited every repo's actual `.git/hooks/post-merge` symlink target and found
**30 non-kubernaut-family repos** wrongly wired to the kubernaut-family
daemon restarter: all 14 dcm-project repos, koku's 3 repos
(`project-koku/koku`, `project-koku/koku-service-operator`,
`insights-onprem/koku`), all 10 praxis-proxy repos, plus engram itself,
`kubernaut-docs`, and `rhdh-plugins`. Every `git pull`/`merge`/`reset`/
`rebase` (and every background `reference-transaction` from any periodic
fetch) in any of these 30 repos was needlessly force-killing
kubernaut-family's shared serena daemon — a second, independent contributor
to the restart-storm symptom beyond the 6 kubernaut-family repos +
`kubernaut-v1.5`/`v1.6` mirror-fetch traffic already fixed above — while
never actually restarting these 30 repos' own per-repo gopls/serena
processes on merge/reset/rebase (only checkout was ever correct for them).
The 6 real kubernaut-family repos (`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-operator`,
`kubernaut-console`, `kubernaut-demo-scenarios`, `kubernaut-v1.5`,
`kubernaut-v1.6`) were confirmed correctly wired and left untouched.

**Fix**: added `git-hooks/generic/post-merge-generic-mcp.sh` and
`reference-transaction-generic-mcp.sh` (family-agnostic, per-repo
`_restart-gopls.sh`/`_restart-serena.sh` only); corrected
`post-checkout-generic-mcp.sh`/`post-checkout-dcm-mcp.sh` to self-provision
from those; deployed both new scripts to `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/`; relinked
all 30 affected repos' `post-merge`/`reference-transaction` symlinks
directly (self-provisioning only fires when the hook is *missing*, so
already-wrong existing symlinks would never have self-healed). Verified via
`readlink` before/after for all 30, plus a spot-check that the 3 sampled
kubernaut-family repos were untouched.

**New templates directory** (`git-hooks/README.md` is the entry point):
`git-hooks/generic/` (ready to symlink, no generation needed) and
`git-hooks/family/*.sh.tmpl` + `git-hooks/generate-hooks.sh` +
`git-hooks/families/kubernaut-family.vars` (a real worked example
documenting the actual, currently-deployed kubernaut-family configuration —
generating from it and diffing against the live deployed script confirmed
functionally identical debounce/restart logic, differing only in a cosmetic
debounce-lockfile filename). `docs/NEW_PROJECT_SETUP.md` step 14 and its File
Checklist table updated to point at this directory instead of assuming
`~/.hindsight/git-hooks/` is pre-populated. All new/modified shell scripts
passed `shellcheck` (POSIX `sh` dialect for the hooks, default for the bash
generator) with no findings beyond one pre-existing, intentional
unused-variable pattern shared with the already-verified deployed scripts.

## 2026-08-16 (same day, fourth follow-up): kubernaut-docs — Serena cross-repo code lookup + broken `core.hooksPath`

**Request**: `kubernaut-docs` needs to compare documentation against actual
code (kubernaut, kubernaut-operator), which requires Serena rather than
CocoIndex-code (kubernaut-docs has no source of its own to index, but *does*
need read access to *other* repos' live symbols).

**Design**: Serena's `--add-mode query-projects` (already enabled on the
shared `io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` daemon) exposes
`query_project`/`list_queryable_projects`, letting an agent read symbols/
references from any *other* registered project without switching its own
active one. This is exactly the "docs vs code" use case, and it's read-only
by construction (no write/edit tools reach the queried project) — a better
fit than adding kubernaut-docs to the write-capable per-repo daemon rotation.

**Setup** (mirrors the existing kubernaut-family per-project pattern):
1. `kubernaut-docs/.serena/project.yml` — `project_name: kubernaut-docs`,
   `language_servers: [markdown]` (its own active-project role is a minor
   markdown-symbol-nav convenience; the real value is `query_project` into
   `kubernaut`/`kubernaut-operator`, already-registered projects).
2. Registered `.../kubernaut-docs` in `~/.serena/serena_config.yml`'s
   `projects:` list.
3. Added `--project kubernaut-docs` to
   `io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family.plist`, giving it a mount
   at `http://127.0.0.1:8893/mcp/kubernaut-docs`.
4. New per-project mcp.json template,
   `~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/kubernaut-family-kubernaut-docs.json`
   (hindsight-docs + hindsight-issues + serena — deliberately **no**
   cocoindex-code entry, since kubernaut-docs has no source for it to index
   and the ask was scoped to Serena only).
5. Converted `kubernaut-docs/.cursor/mcp.json` from a real (non-symlink) file
   into a symlink to that template, and relinked its `.git/hooks/{post-checkout,
   post-merge,reference-transaction}` from the generic per-repo variant to the
   kubernaut-family variant (`post-checkout-cursor-mcp.sh` etc.) so future
   clones/worktrees self-provision the mcp.json symlink and pick up the
   (already-debounced, already-narrowed per the third follow-up above)
   shared-daemon restart-on-checkout behavior.
6. Restarted **both** `io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family`
   (bootout+bootstrap, since its `--project` list — a `ProgramArguments`
   change — needed picking up) **and** the underlying
   `io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` + `io.vectorize.serena-project-server`
   daemons (`kickstart -k` sufficient here, since only the external
   `serena_config.yml`/`.serena/project.yml` files changed, not their own
   `ProgramArguments`) — the shared serena daemon loads its project registry
   at startup, so editing the YAML alone doesn't take effect until it
   restarts. First verification attempt (multiplex-only restart) reproduced
   this exactly: `initialize` succeeded (multiplex-level, project-agnostic)
   but the very next call failed with `"Session not found"` — the multiplex's
   per-call `activate_project(kubernaut-docs)` against the *stale* upstream
   daemon errored out and killed the session. Restarting the upstream daemon
   too resolved it.
7. Updated `cursor/kubernaut-docs-hindsight-memory.mdc` — replaced the
   2026-08-16 "no code-intelligence" scope note (now inaccurate) with the new
   scope and a "Verifying docs against actual code" section showing
   `query_project` usage; redeployed via `check-rule-sync.py --fix` (which, as
   a side effect, also flushed an unrelated pre-existing pending drift on the
   `operator` rule pair).

**Verification**: full MCP session against the new mount confirmed
`list_queryable_projects` returns all 15 registered projects including
`kubernaut`/`kubernaut-operator`, and `query_project(project_name=
"kubernaut-operator", tool_name="get_symbols_overview", tool_params_json=
"{\"relative_path\": \"cmd/main.go\"}")` returned real symbol data
(`operatorFlags` struct, `main`/`parseFlags`/etc. functions) — confirming the
cross-repo read path is live end-to-end, not just configured.

**Separate bug found and fixed along the way**: `kubernaut-docs`'s
`core.hooksPath` git config was set to an **absolute, non-existent** path
(`/Users/.../kubernaut-docs/.githooks`) — unlike the sibling kubernaut-family
repos, which either leave it unset (`kubernaut` itself) or use a *relative*
`.githooks` value (`kubernaut-operator`, `engram`). Since that directory
never existed, none of kubernaut-docs's `.git/hooks/*` symlinks — including
the ones correctly relinked to the generic scripts during the third
follow-up's 30-repo fix, above — were ever actually firing. Fixed by
`git config --unset core.hooksPath`, restoring the default `.git/hooks/`
location where the correct symlinks already lived. Root cause of the stray
absolute-path config is unknown (likely a one-off manual `git config` typo
during initial onboarding); worth a quick spot-check of other repos'
`core.hooksPath` values if a similar "hooks silently not firing" symptom
shows up elsewhere.

**Scope note**: deliberately did not add `cocoindex-code` to kubernaut-docs
(only asked for Serena, and it has no source of its own for that tool to
index). If semantic/pattern doc-vs-code search across kubernaut's codebase
from kubernaut-docs is wanted later, that's a separate, larger change (would
mean pointing at the existing `engram-search-kubernaut` shared code-search
daemon rather than provisioning a new one).

## 2026-08-16 (same day, fifth follow-up): live incident — `query_project` against an interactive dev clone silently returned stale branch state; fixed with pinned read-only mirror projects

**Incident**: within minutes of the fourth follow-up above landing, a
`kubernaut-operator`-workspace agent session queried Serena's
`query_project(project_name="kubernaut", ...)` for a field (`HealthURL` in
`internal/config/datastorage.go`) added by a just-merged PR (#2168 on
`main`). The query returned nothing, matching a pre-#2168 state, even though
the PR was confirmed merged on GitHub. The agent correctly diagnosed the
cause itself before escalating: `list_queryable_projects` confirmed
`kubernaut` resolves to the shared clone at
`~/go/src/github.com/jordigilh/kubernaut`, but that clone's working tree was
checked out on `release/v1.5` (commit `3592e698`) — an *older* line that
never had the change — not `main`. The agent declined to unilaterally
`git checkout main` on a clone another window/session might depend on being
at `release/v1.5`, and escalated for a human go-ahead instead.

**Root cause**: none of the kubernaut-family's Serena-registered "project"
clones (`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-operator`, `kubernaut-console`,
`kubernaut-v1.5`, `kubernaut-v1.6`) are actually pinned to their namesake
branch — they're ordinary interactive working directories that also happen
to be Serena-registered, freely branch-switched for whatever feature/fix
work is active at any given moment (verified: at incident time,
`kubernaut-v1.5`'s clone was on `feat/1985-readyz-shared-port-8080` and
`kubernaut-v1.6`'s was on `feat/2162-gateway-enabled-toggle` — neither was
even on its own namesake release branch). Serena's `query_project` is
purely LSP-live: it reads whatever bytes are on disk in the target
project's directory at call time, with no persisted multi-branch index of
its own — unlike CocoIndex, which decouples "what's indexed" from "what's
currently checked out" by ingesting from dedicated mirror worktrees into a
Postgres/pgvector index at ingestion time. This is a structural mismatch:
the "which branch does `kubernaut` mean" question has one obviously correct
answer for interactive within-repo work (whatever a human is actively
working on) but a different, incompatible one for cross-repo verification
queries (a specific, guaranteed branch) — the two use cases were sharing the
same registered project names.

**Immediate fix (unblocking the escalated session)**: verified the
`kubernaut` clone was safe to touch (no in-progress merge/rebase, no
unpushed commits on `release/v1.5` — in sync with `origin/release/v1.5`, no
stash entries tied to that checkout), switched it to `main`, pulled (landed
at `9b8094d`, confirmed PR #2168 and the `HealthURL` field present), and
restarted `io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` +
`io.vectorize.serena-project-server` (`kickstart -k`) to flush any
LSP-cached symbol state from the pre-switch checkout. Re-ran the escalated
agent's exact `query_project` call afterward and confirmed it now returns
the field correctly.

**Structural fix (closing the gap so it can't recur)**: rather than adding
new dedicated clones that would themselves need manual upkeep, reused
existing, already-auto-synced infrastructure — the `watch-mirrors-config.sh`
/ `watch-mirrors-lib.sh` mirror worktrees built for CocoIndex
(`~/.hindsight/watch/<name>`, each a detached-HEAD worktree tracking one
branch, refreshed every 10 minutes by the existing
`io.vectorize.cocoindex.watch-sync` launchd job via `fetch` +
`reset --hard`) were simply also registered as Serena projects:

- Added `.serena/project.yml` (`read_only: true`, `project_name:
  "<name>-mirror"`) to each of the 7 currently-existing mirror worktrees:
  `kubernaut` (main), `kubernaut-release-v1.5`, `kubernaut-operator` (main),
  `kubernaut-operator-release-v1.5`, `kubernaut-console` (main, typescript),
  `kubernaut-console-release-v1.5` (typescript), `kubernaut-demo-scenarios`
  (main, no release-line mirror exists for this repo). `release/v1.6`
  mirrors don't exist yet (upstream branch not cut per
  `watch-mirrors-config.sh`'s comment) — will need the same treatment once
  `ensure_mirror()` creates them.
- Registered all 7 paths in `~/.serena/serena_config.yml`'s `projects:`
  list.
- Restarted only `io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` +
  `io.vectorize.serena-project-server` (`kickstart -k`) — **not** the
  multiplex (`io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family`), since
  `query_project`/`list_queryable_projects` read the shared daemon's *full*
  project registry regardless of whether a project has its own multiplex
  mount (confirmed: pre-existing non-family projects like `koku` and
  `praxis-ai`, which have no kubernaut-family mount at all, were already
  reachable via `list_queryable_projects` before this change) — so no
  `--project` / `ProgramArguments` change was needed on the multiplex
  itself.
- Verified end-to-end with a differential test that doubles as proof the
  fix actually works, not just that it's configured: queried both
  `kubernaut-release-v1.5-mirror` and `kubernaut-mirror` for `HealthURL` in
  the same file — the v1.5 mirror correctly returned `{}` (empty, PR #2168
  doesn't exist on that line) while the main mirror correctly returned the
  full match, confirming the two pinned mirrors reliably diverge exactly as
  their branches do.
- Updated `cursor/kubernaut-docs-hindsight-memory.mdc` (its "Verifying docs
  against actual code" section, added in the fourth follow-up above) and
  `cursor/operator-hindsight-memory.mdc` to direct agents at the
  `-mirror`-suffixed project names instead of the ambiguous bare repo names
  whenever a guaranteed branch state is needed; redeployed via
  `check-rule-sync.py --fix`.

**Naming convention going forward**: `<repo>-mirror` for `main`,
`<repo>-release-v1.X-mirror` for release lines — e.g. `kubernaut-mirror`,
`kubernaut-release-v1.5-mirror`, `kubernaut-operator-mirror`,
`kubernaut-operator-release-v1.5-mirror`, `kubernaut-console-mirror`,
`kubernaut-console-release-v1.5-mirror`, `kubernaut-demo-scenarios-mirror`.
The un-suffixed names (`kubernaut`, `kubernaut-operator`, etc.) remain the
interactive dev clones — unchanged, still freely branch-switchable, still
the right choice for working *on* that repo directly; only cross-repo
"give me a specific branch's state" queries should prefer the mirrors.

## 2026-08-17: koku-family Serena cross-repo misrouting — migrated to shared HTTP daemon (like kubernaut-family)

**Symptom (reported by the team managing koku-service-operator)**:
`project-koku/koku` is Python, its sibling `project-koku/koku-service-operator`
is Go; from a `koku-service-operator` session, Serena's `get_current_config`
reported "ready" but resolved to the *Python* `koku` project — no `go.mod`,
couldn't find `internal/resources/envoy.go`.

**Root cause**: both repos used the older, per-window pattern — each
`.cursor/mcp.json` spawns its own `uvx --from git+https://github.com/oraios/serena
serena start-mcp-server --project ${workspaceFolder} ...` stdio subprocess,
keyed only by the literal, identically-named `"serena"` MCP server entry
(the command template is byte-identical across every repo using this
pattern; only the runtime-substituted `${workspaceFolder}` differs). With
both `koku` and `koku-service-operator` open around the same time, Cursor's
stdio-MCP-client most likely pooled/reused one already-spawned process
across both windows instead of spawning a second one — matching exactly
what kubernaut-family hit and fixed on 2026-08-13 (see that day's entries)
before moving to a shared HTTP daemon with explicit per-project mounts.
Confirmed empirically on this machine: at the time of investigation, only
one `koku`-scoped (not `koku-service-operator`-scoped) stdio serena process
was running, and none scoped to `koku-service-operator` existed at all.

**Scope check**: this per-window stdio pattern is used by 25 repos total
(`dcm-project`'s 14, `praxis-proxy`'s 7, `project-koku`'s 2,
`insights-onprem/koku`, `rhdh-plugins`). Asked the user whether to fix
broadly or narrowly; decision: only `kubernaut-family` and `koku-family` are
multi-language *and* multi-repo, which is what actually makes the failure
mode (wrong language entirely, not just wrong file paths within the same
language) severe — `dcm-project`/`praxis-proxy`/`rhdh-plugins` are
mono-language families, deliberately left on the stdio pattern for now.

**Fix — mirrored the kubernaut-family shared-daemon architecture exactly**:
- `launchd/io.vectorize.serena.koku-family.plist`: shared serena daemon,
  port 8894, no `--project` (keeps `activate_project` available), `--add-mode
  query-projects`. One process serves both languages fine — language
  servers are spawned per-project from each project's own
  `.serena/project.yml` `language_servers:` field, not fixed at daemon
  startup.
- `launchd/io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.koku-family.plist`: reused the
  existing, already-generic `engram-serena-multiplex` module unchanged
  (its own docstring: "Not tied to 'kubernaut-family' specifically") —
  port 8895, `--upstream-url http://127.0.0.1:8894/mcp`, `--project koku
  --project koku-service-operator`. Gives each repo a fixed mount
  (`/mcp/koku`, `/mcp/koku-service-operator`) instead of relying on
  whichever repo last called `activate_project`.
- `io.vectorize.serena-project-server` (port 24225, backs `query_project`)
  needed **no changes** — already documented as family-agnostic, shared
  by any serena daemon.
- Registered `project-koku/koku-service-operator`'s path in
  `~/.serena/serena_config.yml`'s `projects:` list (`koku` was already
  registered).
- **Found and fixed a second, pre-existing, previously-harmless bug while
  verifying**: `insights-onprem/koku` (an unrelated downstream fork) had
  `project_name: "koku"` in its own `.serena/project.yml` — an exact
  collision with `project-koku/koku`'s own `project_name`. This never
  mattered while every repo ran an isolated per-window stdio process
  (nothing ever resolved a *name* to a path), but broke name resolution the
  moment `project-koku/koku` joined a shared daemon that does resolve names
  — the `/mcp/koku` mount returned "Internal Server Error" until the fork
  was renamed to `project_name: "koku-insights-onprem"`.
- Converted both repos' `.cursor/mcp.json` from regular files to symlinks
  into new per-project templates
  (`~/.hindsight/cursor-mcp-templates/koku-family-koku.json`,
  `...-koku-service-operator.json`) — same convention as kubernaut-family,
  only the `"serena"` entry changed (from stdio to
  `http://127.0.0.1:8895/mcp/<project>`); `hindsight-docs`/`hindsight-issues`/
  `cocoindex-code` entries left untouched.
- Added `git-hooks/families/koku-family.vars` and generated
  `~/.hindsight/git-hooks/{post-checkout,post-merge,reference-transaction}-koku-family-mcp.sh`
  + `_restart-koku-family-daemons.sh` via `git-hooks/generate-hooks.sh`
  (`DAEMON_LABELS="io.vectorize.serena.koku-family io.vectorize.serena-project-server"`,
  excluding the multiplex, same reasoning as kubernaut-family's vars file:
  only daemons with real per-checkout file-state staleness risk belong in
  the debounced restart path). Re-symlinked both repos' `.git/hooks/{post-checkout,post-merge,reference-transaction}`
  from the generic single-repo variant to this family variant.
- Updated `cursor/koku-hindsight-memory.mdc` (deployed to both repos) to
  document the shared daemon, generalized its "three-tier code guidance"
  section to cover both Python (Serena=Pyright) and Go
  (Serena=gopls), and added a `koku-service-operator` entry to
  `check-rule-sync.py`'s `RULE_PAIRS` (same canonical source as `koku`,
  separate deployed copy — unlike the koku forks, which share content by
  manual copy, `koku-service-operator` is a genuinely independent repo with
  its own git history).

**Verification (not just configured — proven working)**: initialized real
MCP sessions against both new mounts and called `get_current_config` —
`/mcp/koku` reported `Active project: koku`, `/mcp/koku-service-operator`
reported `Active project: koku-service-operator`, no cross-contamination.
Followed up with real `get_symbols_overview` calls: `koku-service-operator`'s
`cmd/wait-for/main.go` correctly returned Go-shaped symbols (`Function`,
`Struct`, `Constant`); `koku`'s `dev/scripts/delete_glue.py` correctly
returned Python-shaped symbols (`Function`). Confirms gopls and Pyright are
both live and correctly scoped on the one shared daemon.

## 2026-08-20: `serena-multiplex` cache goes stale across the shared daemon's ~10-minute restart cycle, breaking any long-lived session

**Symptom**: a `kubernaut-operator` Cursor window reported Serena "not
reporting anything" — tool calls silently failed. Log evidence
(`~/.hindsight/logs/serena-kubernaut-family-stderr.log`) showed repeated
`ToolCallError: No active project` for session IDs that had *already*
successfully called `activate_project` earlier in the same session.

**Root cause, confirmed by exact timestamp correlation**: the watch-mirror
sync (`io.vectorize.cocoindex.watch-sync`, `StartInterval: 600`) runs
`git fetch origin <branch>` in every kubernaut-family **live clone** every
10 minutes to refresh CocoIndex's mirror worktrees. Each fetch fires the
`reference-transaction` git hook installed in the live clones
(`reference-transaction-cursor-mcp.sh` → `_restart-kubernaut-family-daemons.sh`
→ `launchctl kickstart -k io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family`) — by
design, per that hook's own header comment, to flush stale in-memory
LSP/symbol state after any ref-changing git operation, mirror-refresh
fetches included. Verified: the `service inactive: io.vectorize.serena.
kubernaut-family` unified-log entry at `23:19:04.327` matches, to the
second, `watch-mirrors-sync.log`'s `23:19:04 - synced kubernaut` entry, and
this pairing repeats every single ~10-minute sync cycle (checked 8+
consecutive cycles).

That `kickstart -k` restart wipes **all** in-process Serena state on the raw
upstream daemon (port 8892), including which project(s) have had
`activate_project` called. `io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family`
(port 8893 — what every repo's `.cursor/mcp.json` actually points at) is
deliberately *not* restarted alongside it (`_restart-kubernaut-family-
daemons.sh`'s header: "a stateless HTTP relay ... dropped from this script
entirely"). That classification is subtly wrong: `serena_multiplex.py`'s
`ActiveProjectTracker.active` field caches which project the multiplex
itself last activated, and `pinned()` only re-activates when a *different*
project is requested (`if self.active != project`). After an upstream
restart, the multiplex's cache still says e.g. `"kubernaut-operator"` is
active — nothing told it otherwise — so it skips re-activation and forwards
straight into a fresh upstream process with no active project at all.
Since any real editing session easily outlasts 10 minutes, every
kubernaut-family Serena session was expected to break on this cycle and
stay broken (only a *different* project's use of the multiplex in the
meantime would incidentally reset the cache — and only for that other
project, not the one that broke).

**Fix**: `serena_multiplex.py` — added `ActiveProjectTracker.invalidate()`
and a `_forward_scoped()` wrapper that inspects the forwarded response body
for the "No active project" tool-error signature
(`_looks_like_no_active_project_error()`) and, if found, invalidates the
cache and retries exactly once (forcing a real re-activation) before
returning. `build_app()`'s endpoint now calls `_forward_scoped()` instead of
the bare `async with tracker.pinned(...)` for the "scoped" tool-call path.
One retry only — a second identical failure is returned as-is rather than
looping, so a genuinely-down upstream still surfaces as an error instead of
hanging. 10 new unit tests added to `tests/test_serena_multiplex.py`
(TDD: written first, confirmed RED against the pre-fix code, then GREEN);
full 32-test module suite passes. Deployed by restarting both
`io.vectorize.serena-multiplex.kubernaut-family` and `...koku-family`
(same shared module) — both are editable-installed from this repo's source,
so the restart alone picked up the fix, no reinstall needed. Verified live
against the real `kubernaut-operator` mount post-restart.

**Not fixed, deliberately out of scope**: the underlying ~10-minute restart
cadence itself is unchanged — it's an intentional trade-off (flush
potentially-stale LSP state after every mirror-refresh fetch) documented in
`_restart-kubernaut-family-daemons.sh`'s own header, and this fix makes that
cadence safe to keep rather than trying to eliminate it. If restart
*frequency* itself ever needs revisiting (e.g. only restarting when a mirror
fetch actually fast-forwards, not on every poll), that's a separate,
narrower follow-up.

**Follow-up decision, same investigation: `--add-mode no-memories` stays on
the shared daemon.** Operator-team feedback bundled `search_for_pattern`,
`get_symbols_overview`, and `list_memories` together as "broken on this
mount." Live-tested all three against the real `kubernaut-operator` mount
post-fix: `search_for_pattern`/`get_symbols_overview` are the same
stale-cache bug above and now work correctly; `list_memories` fails
separately and permanently with `Tool 'list_memories' is not active` because
the shared `io.vectorize.serena.kubernaut-family` daemon has always been
started with `--add-mode no-memories` (excludes `write_memory`,
`read_memory`, `delete_memory`, `edit_memory`, `rename_memory`,
`list_memories`, `onboarding`) — no comment or findings entry ever
documented why, and no retry logic can fix it since the tool is absent from
the daemon's active tool set, not failing.

Investigated re-enabling it. Ruled out two initial concerns as unfounded:
- **CocoIndex ingestion collision**: checked `src/engram/flows/kubernaut.py`
  directly — every docs `walk_dir` is scoped to a `docs/`-style
  *subdirectory* of each mirror worktree (e.g. `operator_dir / "docs"`),
  never the repo root where `.serena/` actually lives; every code
  `walk_dir` only matches `**/*.go`/`**/*.ts`/`**/*.tsx`, never `.md`. Zero
  literal `.serena` reference anywhere in the ingestion code. No collision.
- **Hindsight naming collision**: Serena's memories (verified against
  `serena/tools/memory_tools.py`) are plain per-project `.md` files under
  `.serena/memories/`, written/read only when the agent explicitly decides
  to via `write_memory`/`read_memory`/`list_memories` — a lightweight,
  agent-authored note-taking system with no search, no LLM extraction, no
  embeddings. Hindsight's memory system is unrelated: automatically mined
  nightly from transcripts/PRs via an LLM pipeline, stored in
  Postgres/pgvector banks, retrieved by semantic search. Different storage,
  different trigger, no shared code path — just a naming coincidence.

**But a third, real concern killed it**: Serena memories are branch-agnostic
-- `save_memory`/`load_memory` in `memory_tools.py` are pure name-based file
reads/writes with no commit/branch awareness at all. The shared daemon's
live per-repo mounts (e.g. `kubernaut-operator`) get their branches switched
routinely during normal dev work (confirmed happening mid-session tonight:
`checkout main → pull → checkout feat/368-agentsession-crd-rbac` in the
reflog). A memory written on one branch (e.g. "function `Foo` is defined at
`rbac.go:50`") would silently persist and keep being served as fact after
switching to a branch where it's no longer true. Unlike the stale-cache bug
this entry fixes, there's no existing flush mechanism that would catch this
— the periodic daemon restart clears LSP/active-project state, but does
nothing to memory files sitting on disk. This is a strictly worse failure
mode than a stale tool error: a stale memory is silently, confidently wrong
context fed straight into the agent instead of a visible failure. Would be
safe on the read-only, single-branch-pinned mirror projects (they never
switch branches), but not on the primary live per-repo mounts operator team
actually uses.

**Decision: leave `--add-mode no-memories` as-is.** No config change made.
Revisit only if Serena ever gains per-branch/per-commit memory scoping, or
if a mount-specific override becomes worth the complexity for the
mirror-only (never-branch-switching) projects specifically.

---
